- **No more two-second wait for ChatGPT's voice.** When the OpenAI account has no credit the voice now says so at once (one try, not one per speech model) and is left alone for three minutes, then Eya's own on-device voice speaks.
- **Gemini's quota wording is no longer called "credit".**

**Verified:** unit tests (which way to talk for each state, the badge, the once-only sentence and when it repeats, the sentence in front of an answer, choosing clearing only that AI's rest, a test marking the chain, the voice's single try), and the real app against two fake services on this PC: with a custom service picked, tapping to talk opened the hands-free chat and not Gemini Live and that service answered; with "OpenAI" picked and its account empty, the picker's own Test said so, the route and badge listed it as passed over for credit, the answer began with the sentence and the next one did not; after "credit was added" and the model was picked again, "OpenAI" answered on the next request and was the one in use. **Not verified:** ChatGPT's own answers and voice, because the account behind the saved key still has no credit (checked just now with one test message: HTTP 429 for chat and for speech).

## Several Gemini keys: family and friends, used one after another (22 Oct 2026)

Gemini's free allowance is small and runs out in a few commands. Each person with a Google account can make their own free key, so Eya can now use several: yours, then your father's, your brother's, and so on, one after another.

**Settings → Gemini keys → "More Gemini keys: family and friends".** Add a name and a key (or "Add several at once": one to a line, `Father AIza…`, `Brother AIza…`). Each key is checked with Google, then saved encrypted by Windows for your account only; the page only ever sees a name, the last four characters and how the key stands. Up to 10 extra keys. Your own key (under "Paste your key" or in `.env`) stays first.

- **One after another, by itself.** The first key answers until Google says its free allowance for the day is used up (or it is rate limited, or the key is refused); the same request then goes straight on to the next key, so nothing is seen. A used-up key is left alone until the day's reset (looked at again every three hours at the latest) and then comes back to the front. Only when every key is out does Eya say the daily limit is reached, as before (and then falls to the backup AIs).
- **Everywhere, not only for answers.** Reading documents and the screen, transcribing, searching and the speech voice each move to the next key when their own allowance runs out. The live voice starts on the first key; if it fails or cannot start, the next conversation starts on the next key and Eya restarts it once or twice by herself (never in a loop) before carrying on hands-free.
- **You can see it.** Each key shows "answering now", "ready", "free allowance for today is used up · tried again at 12:45 AM", "slowed down by Google for a moment" or "Google refused this key", with requests answered today. **Test** asks one key a tiny question; a key that works again is put back in use at once, and adding or testing a key wakes Gemini up even if every key had been used up (it used to stay asleep as a whole).
- **Without a key of your own** the first extra key is enough (restart Eya once to switch on documents, the screen and the live voice).
- Which key is resting is kept in a small file with no keys in it (`gemini-key-state.json`); the keys are in `gemini-keys.bin`. `EYA_GEMINI_BASE_URL` points Gemini at another address (a test server).

**Pasting a key.** The first version only accepted a perfectly clean key and answered "I couldn't find a Gemini key in what you pasted" to anything else, with no reason. Now the key is taken out of whatever was pasted (quotes, a name before it, `key=`, invisible characters and no-break spaces from a web page or chat, two keys pasted together, a full stop after it, keys in Google's other style with dots), the name and key boxes are swapped back if they were filled the wrong way round, a key from another service (OpenRouter, Groq…) is recognised and sent to Backup AI instead, and when nothing can be used the message says why: how many characters were pasted and the first four ("AIza"), never the key. The same goes for "Paste your key". Two adds at once (a second press of Add) are merged instead of the last one winning.

Please only add keys people have given you for this, and tell them: usage on their key is theirs (if they ever switch on billing it is billed to them). Google's terms are about each account's own use; you are the one who knows what is fair.

**Verified:** unit tests (the pool: first key until spent, same request on to the next, rate limit, refused key, overload not passed round, every key out gives the daily error, limits and refusals in the right order, fail-fast only for keys that have a next, documents/transcribe/search, rests kept across a restart and dropped on a new day, the live voice and the speech voice moving on, half-spoken sentences never restarted; the pasted lines parser; adding with duplicate / invalid / unreachable / limit / cannot-protect / cannot-write cases; Test messages; keys never sent to the page) and the real app against a fake Gemini on this PC: keys added through the real Settings page (one by name, three pasted at once, one refused by Google, one line with no key), then requests answered Father → Brother (same request) → Mother as each was used up, the list showing who is used up and who is answering, every key out giving the daily-limit message, Test bringing the first key back and the next request using it, the live address moving to the next key after trouble, Remove. **Not verified:** real Google keys (a real daily cap or a real live-voice failure was never reached), so how Google's live voice closes when its allowance is spent is assumed (any unexpected failure moves to the next key).

## Adding to a cart on shops and food apps (22 Oct 2026)

What went wrong: on many shops and food apps Eya got stuck at "add to cart is not showing". Four causes, all in how she found and clicked the button:

- **Every dish's "ADD" was collapsed into one.** The page listing merged controls with the same name, so a menu with thirty "ADD" buttons showed Eya one. The extension (now **0.10.0**) tells the buttons apart by the item they sit under ("ADD — Paneer Butter Masala") and the listing shows them that way.
- **She only matched the exact words.** "Add to cart" now also finds "ADD", "Add to bag", "Add to basket", "Add +", "+" and "Buy now" (never "Add address" or "Add to wishlist").
- **A button that was not showing was "not visible".** Menus and lists that load as you scroll keep the rest further down: a click now scrolls down, up to six screens, looking for it before giving up.
- **Nothing said which item.** `click_on_page` has a new `near` argument (the dish or product). With the same button on the page for several items and none named she no longer clicks the first: she says which items there are and asks (the model names one). A dish that is not on the page is reported as not found, not swapped for one that shares a word.

**You must reload the browser extension once** (chrome://extensions or edge://extensions → Eya Browser Bridge → reload, version 0.10.0). Until then, naming an item answers that the extension is out of date instead of clicking the first button.

**Verified:** unit tests (the matching, synonyms, ambiguity, scrolling in the service, the tool) and the real extension in a real Chrome (`tests/live/cart.live.test.ts`): a food menu with the same ADD under four dishes and a dessert section that only loads on scrolling, and a store page with "Add to bag" beside "Add to wishlist" and "Add address"; the existing shop and checkout live test still passes (it now has to name which of three shirts). **Not verified:** the real Swiggy, Zomato, Amazon, Flipkart or other live sites, which change their pages often and may hide the button some other way.

## "The page is loading" when it is not (22 Oct 2026)

What went wrong, found while ordering on real apps: after a sign-in, or with an address already chosen, Eya kept saying the page or the address was "loading, wait" when nothing was loading. Three causes:

- **Any busy page counted as loading.** After a click Eya waits for the page to go quiet, up to a few seconds. Real shop and food pages never go quiet (adverts, tickers, carousels), so the wait always ran out and the result said "the page was still loading". Now a page that keeps moving but has finished loading and shows no loading indicator is **ready**; only a page still being built, or one that really shows a spinner, is reported as loading, and the note says which ("a loading indicator was still showing").
- **A progress bar that is part of the page counted as a spinner.** A checkout's "step 2 of 3" bar, a delivery tracker or an upload bar (a progress bar with a value) made every such page "busy" for ever. Loading markers tucked away out of sight (hidden, `aria-hidden`, inert) no longer count either. Only an indicator that is really on screen and has no value does.
- **After the sign-in she looked too early.** When you finished signing in she reported the first half-built page of the redirect. She now keeps looking (up to 15 seconds) until the page has stopped changing, has finished loading and has something to act on, and reports that page.

She is also told, in her instructions, to believe what the page shows: if the address, the item or the button is there she carries on, and she must not say a page or address is loading unless the result says so and a second look still shows nothing usable.

**You must reload the browser extension again** (chrome://extensions or edge://extensions → Eya Browser Bridge → reload, version 0.10.1): the busy-page rule lives in it.

**Verified:** unit tests (the loading notes, the sign-in wait with a half-built page then the real one, a page that never finishes), and the real extension in a real Chrome on a page with a ticker, a rotating advert, a checkout progress bar with a value, hidden loading markers and a real seven-second spinner (`tests/live/busy.live.test.ts`): the busy page is ready at once, the real spinner is reported and then not; the shop and cart live tests still pass. **Not verified:** the real apps you tried: every site has its own ways of showing a loader.

## Eya Browser: her own browser, inside Eya (22 Oct 2026)

**What you asked for:** no separate browser to build or install; use the Chromium that is already in Eya. That is what this is. An earlier version of this work drove your system Chrome/Edge through Playwright; that was removed (`playwright-core` is no longer a dependency, nothing in Eya starts Chrome or Edge for Eya Browser).

**How it works.** Eya Browser is a set of tabs (Electron `WebContentsView`s) with a profile of their own (`persist:eya-browser`, so sign-ins you do there stay between tasks). The Browser page in the Eya window reports the rectangle it has for the browser and the app draws the live page exactly there, so you watch the real page, can click and type in it, and it has an address bar, Back, Forward, Reload, Stop loading, New tab and a tab strip. While another page of the app is showing, the tabs stay alive out of sight and Eya keeps working in them. It speaks the same request language as the browser extension (`open_url`, `observe`, `click`, `fill`…) and runs the very same page script, so there is one implementation of "look at a page / click / type" for Eya Browser and for your own browser.

**Which browser a request uses** (`browserRouter.ts`, never by asking): Eya Browser by default; your own browser when you say so ("my Chrome", "my browser", "the tab I have open", "my logged-in account"), for chat apps (WhatsApp etc.), or when you chose it in Settings; a task already under way stays where it is. If you ask for your own browser and it is not connected she says so and offers Connect My Browser; she never quietly uses Eya Browser instead.

**The Browser page** shows where the task is working, the goal, the steps done, what is holding it up, and an activity log; controls: Pause / Resume, Stop, Take over (you click and type in the page yourself, Eya waits; your passwords never pass through Eya), Hand back, Open in my browser, Connect / Disconnect My Browser, and the choice of default browser.

**Recovery, in words not codes** (`navigationErrors.ts`): a name that does not exist, a refused connection, a redirect loop, a certificate problem and a blocked site are explained and not retried; a timeout, a server error (5xx), a page replaced mid-load or a crashed page get one more try; a site's own 403 / 404 / 429 is reported; a redirect to a different site is called out loudly; a blank page is given time to build itself before it is called blank; a file that downloads is saved to Downloads and only reported when it is really on disk; a page's new window opens as a new tab and is followed; an `alert`/`confirm` is dismissed (never accepted) and mentioned; the location, camera, microphone and notifications are never granted; only http(s) addresses open (never a file on this computer); a closed or crashed tab is opened again at the next step. Something Eya cannot find on the page waits for you to say where to click and carries on from the same page. A CAPTCHA is only watched, never solved: Eya waits and carries on when you have done it. Passwords and card numbers are never typed by Eya.

**Test-only switches** (never needed in normal use): `EYA_BROWSER_DOWNLOADS`, `EYA_BROWSER_NAV_TIMEOUT_MS`, `EYA_NO_BROWSER_LAUNCH=1` (a test must not start the real Chrome/Edge), `EYA_NO_MIC=1` (a test copy must not react to the room), `EYA_LOG_LEVEL=debug` (logs each page load, commit and failure).

**Verified (22 Oct 2026):** typecheck, the full unit suite (2533 passed) including 27 tests of the browser host with fake tabs, and a real-Electron run through the same tool path the live voice uses against a local site made to misbehave (63 checks: redirects, a redirect to another site, a page that fills itself in later, 403/404, unknown name, refused connection, redirect loop, slow and flaky pages, a site that never answers, a file address refused, numeric form, CAPTCHA pause and resume, sign-in with the password left to the user, expired session, a missing button then guidance, a verified download, alert/confirm, a new window, location refused, Pause/Resume/Stop/Take over, address bar, Back/Forward/Reload/Stop loading, tabs, working while another app page shows, every tab closed, a crashed page, no Chrome/Edge process started). It was run from source and from the built app. The one check that depends on the Eya window not being covered by another program (the browser reporting itself visible after coming back to its page) fails when another window is in front, and passed otherwise.

**Not verified / limits:** real shopping and food sites (Swiggy, Zomato, Amazon, Flipkart) were not run in the embedded browser; a page's own pop-up window opens as a tab, so a sign-in that relies on the pop-up talking back to the page that opened it ("Sign in with Google" in a pop-up) may not complete there (use My Browser for those); video sites with DRM may not play in Electron's Chromium; what happens when the whole Eya window is hidden or minimised during a task was not tested; the connection of My Browser through the extension for the new panel is unit-tested with fakes only.

## The Eya window as a dashboard, with every part hideable, and the workspace beside Eya (22 Oct 2026)

**What you asked for:** the Eya desktop window like the dashboard picture you sent, each section able to be hidden and shown again, and — when Eya works on a website — one half of the window Eya and the other half her browser doing the work, the way Manus or Gemini's agent shows a task.

**The look.** A light window by default (the sun/moon button at the top right switches to the dark one; the choice is remembered). Left: Eya's name and face, the screens (Home, Tasks, Chat, Browser, Live Screen, Files, Memory, Settings), the chat apps and whether each is allowed, and which AI is answering. Top: an ask box ("Ask Eya anything… or give a task", **Ctrl+K** jumps to it), notifications (tasks waiting for you), light/dark, the Layout menu, your name, and the window buttons. Home (since 23 Oct 2026 the middle is a conversation workspace, see "The chat workspace" below; this paragraph describes the first version): the greeting for the hour and your Windows name, the big ask box with its shortcuts, **Your Agent at Work** (the steps of the task now being done, with Stop), Recent Tasks (what Eya did, with a button to do it again), Quick Actions, and on the right Tasks in progress, Popular Apps (each opens in Eya Browser beside Eya), Recent Files (click to open) and Today's Activity (tasks finished today against yesterday, hour by hour). **Gemini keys** is a card on Home too: your own key and the ones from family or friends, which one is answering now and which are used up for today, with "Add or manage keys" (Settings → Gemini keys); the AI picker is under the greeting, and the AI card in the sidebar opens the keys as well. The keys are used one after another: when one runs out for the day the next takes over by itself (checked in the real app against a fake Google, see tests/live/gemini-keys-e2e.mjs). The window opens bigger (1360 x 860, smaller on a small screen).

**Hide and show.** Every card has a small eye-off button (faint until you point at it) that hides it; the sidebar's Connected Apps and AI status hide the same way. The **Layout** button in the top bar lists every section with a switch, and "Show everything" brings them all back; a thin row under a column offers the hidden ones too. A column with nothing left in it closes up. What is hidden is remembered between runs (in the window's own storage; if storage is blocked, the choice lasts until the window closes).

**The workspace.** When a task starts working on a website in Eya Browser, the app comes up on the Browser screen by itself, once per task: **Eya on the left** (her face and status, Pause / Stop / Take over, the task, the steps as they happen with a marker on the current one, what is holding her up, what she says, and a box to tell her what to do next, or the microphone) and **her browser on the right** (the real page, address bar, Back / Forward / Reload / Stop loading, tabs, "Open in my browser"). The panel button hides Eya's side to give the browser the whole window, and the same button brings it back. At the bottom right: the switch **"Open beside Eya automatically"** (off: tasks never take over the screen), Connect / Disconnect My Browser, and which browser Eya uses. Tasks that run in your own browser (My Browser) do not open the split. To make this possible Eya now says which browser a request uses *before* the page opens, not after.

**Verified:** typecheck, the full unit suite, and the real app (75 checks: the dashboard's sections, hide / bring back from the Layout menu, a hidden sidebar part remembered, the workspace opening by itself with Eya on the left and the browser (the larger half) on the right, Eya's panel hiding and showing, the switch turning the automatic opening off, and everything the browser checks covered before). Light and dark were looked at on Home, Settings, Files, Chat, Tasks, Memory and Live Screen.

**Not done / limits:** "Agents", "Knowledge", "Integrations" and "Automation" of the picture are not screens here, because those features do not exist; the sidebar lists the screens Eya really has (Memory holds what Eya has learned, Settings holds the apps and privacy). The Connected Apps list is the chat apps you may allow (Allowed / Off), not a list of accounts. Popular Apps are plain coloured tiles, not the companies' logos. The divider between the two halves is fixed (hide Eya's side for a full-width browser).

## Local AI: Ollama with llama3.2:1b, on this PC (23 Oct 2026)

**What it is.** A small AI that runs on the PC through Ollama: no key, no account, and what you ask it is not sent to any cloud AI. Eya already had an Ollama provider, but only as a last resort that nobody could see or choose, with no streaming and an old default model (`llama3.1`, which is not the one installed: the `.env` still said so, so it could never have answered). It is now a proper choice.

**Set it up (Windows).** Install Ollama, then once: `ollama pull llama3.2:1b`. In Eya: **Settings → Local AI (Ollama)** (or the AI list at the top of Home and Backup AI). That page says whether Ollama is running and has the model, and what to do if not (open the Ollama app or run `ollama serve`; `ollama pull llama3.2:1b`), lists the installed models to choose from, and has **Check again**. Three ways to use it: **Automatic** (as before: Gemini first; the local AI only when the others cannot answer), **Local AI first** (everyday chat is answered here; a request that is too much for a small model — long writing, research, summaries, work on websites and accounts — is handed to a cloud AI), and **Local AI only** (no cloud AI is ever asked to answer; if Ollama is stopped you are told so plainly). The choice is remembered. The AI card in the sidebar and the badge say "Local AI" and the model when it is answering, and, if you chose it and it is not running, say so next to the AI that answers instead.

**How it is wired.** `src/main/providers/ai/OllamaAIProvider.ts` is one more provider in the same chain as Gemini and the backup AIs (`ChainedAIProvider`): no second chat system. Everyday commands (open an app, a folder, a site, find a file) never reach any AI: Eya's own fast layer does them first. For chat it talks to `/api/chat`, streaming: the answer is shown word by word in the Chat screen and the quick panel (`AgentUpdate.partial`), never spoken until whole. The conversation is kept: the last few lines go with each question, so a follow-up is understood.

**Why the prompt is short.** On this laptop (Core i3, 8 GB) the model reads about 43 words a second and writes 8 to 10, so Eya's long instructions for the cloud AIs would be a minute of silence. `localPrompt.ts` replaces them with a three-line instruction (plus the language rule and the date and time), the last six earlier lines (each cut short), and, only when you ask for something to be done, a dozen safe everyday tools with short descriptions. Measured with exactly that request: first start 6.5 s (the model loads in 4 s), then about half a second to the first word, 1 to 2 s for a short answer, about 9 s for three sentences. The model stays loaded for 30 minutes (`keep_alive`), with a 2048-word context and half the PC's threads so the window stays smooth (the same options for every request, or Ollama loads the model again: measured 3 to 11 s each time they changed). It is only loaded ahead of the first question when you have chosen the local AI, so it takes no memory while you do not.

**Safety.** The model never runs anything. Tool calls it proposes go to Eya's own checked tools, and only tools that were offered to it are accepted (a made-up name is dropped). It is offered opening, finding and a few harmless settings; nothing that deletes, sends, buys, shuts down or writes to a document, so those can only be reached through the cloud AIs or Eya's own commands with their "are you sure" questions. There is no tool that runs a shell command at all. Eya's cloud voice, if you use one, is a separate thing: the words she says aloud still go to that voice service (the offline voice keeps them on the PC).

**Errors, in words.** Ollama not running; model not installed (with the pull command); no answer within 90 s (the model may still be loading); the answer going quiet for 30 s; an answer that never ends (cut off at 3 minutes); something Ollama sent that cannot be read; an empty answer. Each is told to you as it is. When the local AI is first and cannot answer, the next AI answers instead and you are told why, once.

**Verified (23 Oct 2026):** 39 new unit tests (a pretend Ollama on a free port: streaming split anywhere, history, tools offered and dropped, not running, model missing as HTTP 404 and as a stream error, the three timeouts, malformed and empty answers, handing hard requests over, loading the model once, the chain with "first" and "only"); the full suite (2580 passed); and `tests/live/ollama-e2e.mjs` (28 checks) with the real app and the real Ollama and llama3.2:1b: a real answer, shown growing, the page never frozen longer than half a second, a follow-up understood ("What is my name?" answered "Your name is Rakesh."), the date known, a "delete this file" request not carried out, "only" never asking the cloud, "first" handing a research request to the cloud and keeping an ordinary question here, Automatic unchanged, Ollama stopped, model missing, and a model that never answers, with the Settings page checked in each state.

**Known limits.** A 1B model is weak: it can get facts wrong, it will not do long or careful writing, and its tool use is only for simple everyday requests. The first question after Ollama has been idle for half an hour waits several seconds. Whether a request is "too much" for it is judged from its words alone. Memory notes you asked Eya to keep are not given to the local model. Other models in Ollama work (choose one in Settings) but were not tested here.

**Next steps.** Another local model: pull it in Ollama and choose it in Settings → Local AI. Another provider: implement `AIProvider` (`complete(messages, tools, options)`), put it in the chain in `buildAIProvider` (main.ts) and, for a choice in the AI list, give it a value in `aiFirst`. LM Studio or any program that speaks the OpenAI chat format is already possible through Backup AI → Another service.

## Messaging when WhatsApp is installed on the PC (23 Oct 2026)

**Two things found.** (1) Sending in WhatsApp Web stopped working because the add-to-cart change made Eya ask "which one?" for any button that appears twice, and WhatsApp has two Send buttons while a file preview is open: she asked and sent nothing. Now only ADD / Add to cart / Buy buttons are asked about (fixed, with a test that fails without the fix), and the real-Chrome chat tests all pass again. (2) Eya went straight to the web without looking for the installed app.

**What happens now.** Eya looks for WhatsApp for Windows first (its whatsapp: link is registered on the PC). *With a phone number* ("message 98765 43210 I will be late"; ten digits mean +91) she opens the installed app on that chat with the message already in the box and says so; you press Enter. She does not press it, and she does not say it was sent: she cannot see inside the installed app. *With a name* ("message Ravi that ...") she says once that WhatsApp is installed but she cannot type inside it, and uses WhatsApp Web, with the same question before anything is sent.

**Why not the installed app for names.** WhatsApp for Windows is drawn by an embedded web view that shows nothing of its inside to other programs: Windows UI Automation (what Eya uses to click in desktop apps) lists 8 parts of its window, all window frame. Typing into it blind, or choosing a chat by pressing keys, could put a message in the wrong chat with nothing to check it by, so it is not done. Possible later: saved numbers for names (the saved chat names today are names only, on purpose) so that "message Ravi" can open the installed app by number.

**Verified:** 7 new unit tests (number reading, installed with a number, installed with a name once, not installed, the app will not open, Chats off) and `tests/live/whatsapp-installed-e2e.mjs` with the real app and the real WhatsApp for Windows (it came to the front with the message prepared for a number that does not exist; nothing was sent).

## The chat workspace: a conversation, files in the message, chat history, and a side panel only when Eya works (23 Oct 2026)

**What the window is now.** Home opens with two things: the sidebar and the conversation, which fills all the rest (no panels, nothing under it, no Maximize or Panels buttons: the chat needs neither). The middle is a conversation, the way ChatGPT, Claude and Manus work: what you type or say, what Eya answers (growing as it is written when the AI streams) and the files that went with a message. The box under it grows as you write (Enter sends, Shift+Enter starts a new line) and holds the attach button, the AI list, the microphone and Send. The Chat screen is the same workspace.

**One toggle for the sidebar.** A single button in the sidebar's top-right corner (not in the top bar) folds the sidebar to its icons and opens it again; the choice is remembered. Folded, it still has the toggle and New chat as a plus.

**The window buttons: minimize, maximize, close.** Top right of the app window. Minimize and Close put Eya away as the small bubble (she keeps running; only Quit ends her). **Maximize** makes the app fill the whole work area of the screen it is on (everything except the taskbar), with square corners and no edge line; the button then becomes **Restore down**, which gives back the window as it was, in the same place. If you put Eya away as a bubble and open the app again, it comes back maximized if it was left that way, and something that asks for the app while it is open (a task, the tray) does not shrink it. It is drawn by Eya's own code (the window is a transparent, frameless one, which the operating system cannot maximize itself). Checked in the real window by `tests/live/maximize-e2e.mjs` (11 checks) and `tests/windowGeometry.test.ts`.

**New chat and the chat history.** **New chat** (top of the sidebar) starts an empty conversation and clears what Eya remembers of the talk so far, so the new chat starts clean. **Your chats** lists the conversations kept on this PC, newest first, titled from the first thing you said (a search box appears once there are more than 6): click one to carry on with it. Its messages come back, with the names of the files it had, and Eya is given the end of that conversation so a follow-up ("and the date?") is understood. Beside each chat, a pencil renames it and a bin deletes it (it asks first). While Eya is answering, the list cannot be switched under her. Conversations are saved a moment after they change, in `chats.json` in Eya's data folder: what was typed and said, the answers, and only the *names* of attached files (never the files). Up to 200 chats of 200 messages. **Settings → Privacy & Chats** has **Keep my chats** (off: new conversations are not saved; the ones already kept stay until you delete them) and **Delete all chats**.

**The panel on the right: only while a task works.** When Eya starts working on a task (her browser on a job, a task she keeps that is running, or a tool in use — not a plain chat answer), a roomy panel (about a third of the window) opens by itself on the right with **Your Agent at Work** (the question, the step she is on, how it ended) and **Tasks in progress**. It opens only once the task has gone on for about a second (a quick command never opens it) and stays about 5 seconds after the end so the ending can be read. The button at the top bar (only there while there is a panel) closes and opens it; closed, it stays closed for that task and comes back for the next. In a window narrower than about 1020 px it stays away. The other cards (Gemini keys, Popular Apps, Recent Files, Today's Activity, and Recent Tasks under the conversation) are off until you switch them on in the **Layout** menu (which also has **Back to default**); switching one on opens the panel. Nothing was deleted: Gemini keys are also in Settings, files on the Files page, the task history on the Tasks page.

**The browser beside Eya: half and half.** When a task works on a website in Eya Browser, the app opens the workspace by itself: the sidebar folds, **the chat is the left half** (the same conversation and composer, with Pause / Stop / Take over, the task, the step she is on, "Waiting on you", and the steps behind "Show the steps") and **her browser is the right half**, equal widths. Leaving the workspace gives the sidebar back as it was.

**Files in a message.** The clip button, dragging files onto the workspace, or pasting a picture (Ctrl+V) all attach. Several at a time, up to 8, each shown before it is sent with its name, size and kind (a picture shows a small preview) and an x to remove it; the same file twice is added once. Pictures, PDFs, Word (.docx), text, audio and video are accepted, up to 14 MB each. You can ask about the file in the same message, or send with no text and Eya is asked to summarise. A file that cannot be read is refused at once, in words: Excel and PowerPoint ("Save it as a PDF or a .docx"), old .doc, an empty file, one that is too big. Picked and dropped files are read where they are; a pasted picture is kept by Eya in her own folder (`attachments` inside her data folder) and cleared after 3 days.

**How files reach the AI, and what is not pretended.** A message with files goes straight to the AI (the quick commands never see it, so "open notepad" said with a file is not carried out blindly). The AI is told the exact path and reads each file with the existing `analyze_document` tool, one call per file, so nothing is said about a file that was not read. Pictures, PDFs, audio and video are sent to Gemini as they are; a Word file has its text taken out on the PC and is sent as text. Reading needs a Gemini key: without one, or with **Local AI only** chosen, the box says so beside the files and Send stays off. A file you attached is readable even from a folder Eya otherwise keeps away from (a mail program's temporary folder, another drive) because you chose it, but only that exact file, only while Eya runs, and a file named like a secret (`secrets.txt`, `.env`, key files) is still refused.

**Sites you name.** If you tell Eya to open a site, any site (including ones that carry unlicensed films or songs), she opens it and works on its pages as you ask, without refusing, arguing or lecturing; at most one short line ("Okay, opening it. Keep an eye on the screen while I do it."). There is no list of blocked sites in the browser tools. What she still does not do herself is download a cracked program or a pirated copy of a film or song (she says so in one sentence and offers to open the page so you can do it), and she does not go looking for pirate sites on her own (the film and song finder still returns legal places and public-domain copies).

**Verified (23 Oct 2026).** Typecheck; the unit suite, including `tests/attachments.test.ts` and `tests/chatHistory.test.ts` (the store, titles, cleaning, delete/rename/limits, damaged file, opening and starting chats and what Eya remembers); and two real-app runs with a fake Google: `tests/live/workspace-e2e.mjs` (83 checks: only sidebar and chat at the start, one sidebar toggle and folding, no panel for a plain chat, the panel opening for a slow task with the question and the step, closing and reopening it, staying a few seconds then going, extra cards from the Layout menu and Back to default, a narrow window, the file picker, drag and drop, paste, what Google receives for text, Word, picture, audio, PDF and video, Local AI only blocking Send, New chat starting clean, opening an old chat bringing its messages and giving Eya its end, rename, delete with a question, deleting the open chat, Keep my chats off, the Settings rows, the other screens, dark theme) and `tests/live/embedded-e2e.mjs` (the browser workspace: chat left, browser right, equal halves, the sidebar folding and coming back).

**Not verified / limits.** Audio and video were checked only up to the point they are handed to Gemini (the fake Google received the right bytes and type): a real Gemini answer about a real recording was not tried. Files are sent whole, so 14 MB is the ceiling. Excel, PowerPoint and old .doc cannot be read. Without a Gemini key no AI here can read files. Paste and the file picker were driven by scripted events, not a real clipboard or the Windows file dialog. A website the AI model itself declines to work on is still declined by the model (Eya no longer adds a refusal of her own). The side panel's "how it ended" shows only the last finished request of this run. Conversations kept before this update do not exist (the chat used to be in memory only).

## Websites that move, and downloads carried through to the end (24 Oct 2026)

**What was asked.** When a site will not open or has changed its address, Eya should not knock on the same dead address and give up. She should find where the site is now, check it is really that site, carry on, and for "go and download this" finish the job: find the real download control, download, wait until the file is complete, and check it before saying so.

**The module: `src/main/browser/navigationRecovery.ts`** (one reusable service, not logic scattered in tools). It works on the browser Eya already has (the session manager: Eya Browser or the user's own browser) and the existing page notes and error classes (`navigationErrors.ts`). For one site it does, in this order, and changes tactic instead of repeating itself:
1. *Opens the address that is known* and **checks the page is really there**: not blank (a page that builds itself with scripts is looked at again for up to 5 seconds), not an error answer (404, 503…), not an error page or a parked "domain for sale" page, no CAPTCHA. A navigation event alone is not success.
2. *A hiccup* (timeout, busy server, dropped connection, a server error) gets **one more try**; anything that would fail the same way (a name that does not exist) does not.
3. *Looks for the current address*: up to 2 web searches ("<name> official website", "<name> <old host> new address working"). Results are ranked from their own address, title and description, and from a reliable page (an encyclopedia, a government or university page) naming an address. Short links, bare-number addresses, look-alike characters, program files and search engines are never offered.
4. *Opens at most 3 candidates, one at a time, and checks each is the same site*: its title and headings (or its own brand name in its address) must carry the name asked for (all of a one- or two-word name, three quarters of a longer one: "Karnataka High Court" is not "Telangana High Court"). A site that cannot be confirmed is **put to the person, not taken**; one that is clearly something else is dropped.
5. *Redirects* are followed and the destination is checked the same way; a redirect to a link-shortener or an unrelated site is not followed without asking.
6. If words that must be on the page were given (`expect`), they are waited for and then required; a page that loads without them is reported as missing them, not as success.
Limits (all in `DEFAULT_RECOVERY_CONFIG`, changeable): 30 s per page, 75 s for the whole task (inside the 90 s one request is given), 6 pages, 2 searches, 3 candidates, 1 retry. It stops at once at a CAPTCHA or bot check, at a sign-in wall in front of what was asked for, at a site it cannot vouch for, when the person pressed Stop or the browser is not available (reported as that, not as a site problem). The same task asked for twice at once shares one loop. A site of the kind that gives films, songs or programs away without permission is opened if you give its address, but Eya does not go looking for new addresses of such sites.

**Two tools for the model.** `find_and_open_website` (url and/or name, expect words, browser): the module above, answering in plain words what happened (`recovery.notes`: "the saved address did not work; X is the current address and the page was checked"). `download_from_site` (what, url/name, type, confirm): opens the site the same way, follows the site towards the file (a title link, a file's own page, at most 3 steps), picks the real download control (a "Download…" link or button, or a link to a document or media file; programs, off-site "fast download" advert buttons and installers are never offered), **asks the user first** (nothing is downloaded before a yes), then clicks it so the browser saves the file, **waits until the file is finished** (a size that stops growing for 10 s is a failure, not "downloading"), and checks it: it exists, is not empty, is not a program, matches its kind (PDF, ZIP/Office, PNG/JPG/GIF/WebP, MP4, MP3 by their first bytes) and is not a web page saved under a file's name. It reports the real file name, size and folder, and says "downloaded" only then. If clicking starts nothing and the page gives a direct file link on the same site, Eya's own download handler fetches it (same checks). Films and videos: no pirate sites (refused, nothing clicked), a streaming service that only lets you watch or save inside its own app has no normal file (said plainly, never worked around), and a site Eya does not recognise as an official source gets a warning in the question. The system prompt tells the model to use these instead of repeating `open_website` on an address that failed.

**What you see.** "Your Agent at Work" and the Browser page show plain lines: "Opening the requested website…", "The saved address is unavailable. Finding its current address…", "Found an alternative. Verifying the page…", "The page is open. Locating the requested download…", "Download completed. Verifying the saved file…"; a trouble is a crossed step, what only you can settle (a CAPTCHA, a site Eya cannot confirm) is what the task waits for. No browser codes or logs. The history of tasks records the web task like any other.

**Also fixed on the way.** A download whose connection dropped half-way used to stay "interrupted" for ever: the embedded browser now tries to resume it once and, after 4 seconds, cancels it and reports it as failed.

**Verified.** Typecheck; the unit suite (siteIdentity 17, navigationRecovery 21 and siteTools 23 tests: opens first time, a timeout then a retry, an outdated address with a verified alternative, a redirect, a page without the expected content, a search result for the wrong site, a download that fails to finish, a completed and verified one, a CAPTCHA and a sign-in wall, the retry/time/page limits); and `tests/live/recovery-e2e.mjs` (15 checks) in the real app and its real embedded browser against a made-up local internet (`*.localhost` names): a site that works, one that drops its first four visits, an address that answers "not found" while the site lives elsewhere (found through a local stand-in for the search engine, `EYA_TEST_SEARCH_URL`), a redirect, a page without what was asked for, a look-alike site, a CAPTCHA, a site that never answers, a PDF downloaded and checked byte for byte, a download that breaks half-way, a web page saved as a PDF, and a pirate-style site refused.

**Not verified / limits.** The search for a new address was tested against a local stand-in for DuckDuckGo's results page, not the real search engines (their page layout can change). A real CAPTCHA, real sign-in walls and a real movie service were not tried. Identity checking is judged from the address, title, headings and text: a clever copy of a site with the same title can pass, and a real site with an unexpected title is asked about (by design). Downloads that need a sign-in, a payment or a click on an advert are reported as "no download started". Big files that keep growing past 30 seconds are reported as still downloading, not finished. Files of kinds without a known signature (text, CSV, JSON) are checked only for size and for being a web page in disguise.

## Acting like a capable human assistant on the web (24 Oct 2026, part two)

**What was asked.** Use common sense instead of rigid steps: try the address the user gives; if it is wrong, old or down, work out the right one; if there is no address, search with the user's words, look at the results, open the relevant sites and try others; do not ask for what can be found out; once on the right site go on and finish the job; look at what the page really shows, adapt, check the action worked; sensible limits; explain a real blocker.

**What changed (the reasoning and the browser logic, not a second system).**
- **The instructions to the AI** (`prompts.ts`, the browser part) now start with how a capable assistant works: find out yourself instead of asking; the user's address is tried first and the right one worked out when it fails; with no address, search by keywords; opening a site is the start, not the end (carry on until the real request is done); after each step read what changed and, if nothing did or an error showed, change the approach (other wording, another link, the site's own search box, another result, another site: two or three different tries, then explain); say "done" only when the page shows it; say plainly what a real blocker is, what was tried and what would unblock it. The older rules that told her to give up or ask ("ask for the address instead of guessing", "do not try again by yourself", "stop and ask" at the first stuck page) were rewritten to try other routes first.
- **One tool for any "open / go to / find"**: `find_and_open_website` now takes an address, a name, or just **keywords** (what the user wants, in their words). With keywords it searches the web, ranks the results from their titles, descriptions and kind of site, opens them one after another (at most 4, within the same time limits as before) and **keeps the first page that really has what was asked for** (all of up to three words, four fifths of more, so a site's own name on its home page is not enough). A CAPTCHA on one result is skipped for another; only when every one is blocked is it handed to the person. It is forgiving: a bare `example.org/reports` is an address, and words put where an address belongs are treated as what to look for. It is always available (a core tool), and once it has been used the browser tools (click, fill, read…) and their rules are switched on for the rest of the request without the model having to ask.
- **A page that did not open says what to do next**: the failure itself tells the AI not to stop and not to ask the user, but to call `find_and_open_website`.
- **An address typed in a request** ("open example.org") is looked at first (a quick check, 5 seconds at most): one that answers is opened at once in the browser as before; a dead, gone (404/410) or broken (5xx) one is no longer opened blindly: the assistant takes the request and works out the right address. The well-known sites (YouTube, Gmail, Amazon…) open at once without the check.
- **More time for a website job**: a plain request still gets 90 seconds, but once the browser is in use the request gets 3 minutes (finding the site and then doing the task on it takes longer than a chat answer); a job that still does not finish is kept and carried on by itself, as before.
- More everyday words (download, visit, navigate, portal, news, price, score, timetable, notification, admit card, hall ticket, application form) now switch the browser abilities on from the start.

**Limits that stay.** Sensible bounds everywhere (per page, per task, pages opened, searches, candidates, retries). No getting past a CAPTCHA or sign-in; no downloads from sites that give things away without permission and no hunting for their new addresses; a site that cannot be confirmed is put to the person.

**Verified.** Typecheck; the unit suite (2702 tests, including new ones for search-by-keywords: first result wrong then second right, a CAPTCHA on one result, nothing found, limits, pirate sites never offered; the stricter relevance rule; forgiving addresses; the dead-address handover and the instant well-known sites; the failure's hint; the browser group coming with `find_and_open_website`; the longer budget for website jobs; the new instructions present and the old give-up wording gone); and `tests/live/recovery-e2e.mjs` (19 checks) in the real app and its real embedded browser, now also covering: keywords-only search through a local stand-in search page, words in the address field, nothing found, and the real address check on a dead typed address (it is not opened blindly; the assistant takes it).

**Not verified.** How well the real Gemini (or another model) follows the new instructions was not measured (no real model was run: the checks cover the tools, the engine and the instruction text, not the model's choices). The search used the local stand-in, not the real search engines; the address check only looks at the answer code, so a site that returns "200" with an error page is left to the page checks that follow.

## Chat is chat, and the Agent button is for jobs (23 Oct 2026, part three)

**What was asked.** "Hi" or a general question must stay an ordinary conversation: never open the panel on the right, never start the browser. A button beside the message box: **Agent off** = answer in chat; **Agent on** = understand the request, use the browser and the tools, and do the job on her own. Stop refusing from a site's name alone or asking for an address; look first. Take away the extra "Ask Eya anything" box at the top.

**What was really wrong (found by reading the user's own `chats.json` and `tasks.json`).**
1. A request that took longer than 8 seconds was turned into a visible task, and one over 20 seconds was recorded. So a "Hi" that the AI was slow to answer became a "task", and the panel on the right opened because a task was "running".
2. `deep_research` itself answered "ask the user first: do you know a website where this is available?" unless a site was given: she asked for an address before looking at anything.
3. "I'm sorry, I can't open that site" came from the model judging the site's name; nothing was ever opened or looked at.
4. Cards switched on in the Layout menu kept the panel open on their own.

**What changed.**
- **Two modes** (`src/main/agent/agentMode.ts`, `AgentEngine.handleChat/runChat`). The window sends `mode: 'chat' | 'agent'` with every typed message (the Agent button is remembered; it starts off). In chat she is given a short chat prompt and only two tools, `web_search` and `analyze_document` (so a question can be looked up and an attached file read), no tasks, no browser, no files. A message that is a command ("open…", "download…", "send…", "lock…") is not guessed at and not sent to the AI at all: she says at once that it is an Agent job and shows a button, **Turn on Agent and do it**, which switches Agent on and sends the same words as a task. Her reply is shown immediately and spoken meanwhile.
- **A task only when work really starts.** `trackRequest` now turns a request into a task only after a tool began to work (`workStarted`/`isWork`), whatever the time it took. A slow chat answer is never a task. The panel opens only with Agent on **and** a task actually running; the Layout menu's extra cards sit inside that panel and no longer open it.
- **Look before saying no.** `deep_research` no longer asks which site to use; it goes and searches. When she answers "I cannot…" without having used a single tool for something that looks like an action, the engine tells her once to look first (open the site, read what is there, try the legal sources) and only then answer; the prompts say never to refuse from a site's name or the kind of request. Policy kept (corrected 24 Oct: an ordinary site named by name is opened and looked at; a site that gives films away without permission, named WITHOUT a link, is not opened or searched for at all, and an exact link the person sends is opened and looked at but nothing is played or downloaded from it): she does not hunt for such sites' new addresses; she says that plainly, in one sentence, after checking, in one plain sentence, together with the legal route (the film and song finder, YouTube's verified channels, the Internet Archive).
- **The top "Ask Eya anything" box is gone**; Ctrl+K puts the cursor in the message box. The Home suggestions, "Do it again", the quick panel, Recent, Memory and the Tasks card start jobs in Agent mode.

**Verified.** Typecheck; the unit suite (new `tests/agentMode.test.ts`, 15 tests; 3 more in `trackRequest.test.ts`); and in the real app (`tests/live/agent-mode-e2e.mjs`, 17 checks): "Hi" in normal chat, even with a 9.5-second AI, creates no task, opens no panel, touches no browser and gets only the chat prompt and the two chat tools; a command typed in chat is answered with the hint and the button, no AI call; the button turns Agent on and a real task opens Eya's browser beside the chat; with Agent on "Hi" still makes no task; the movie-site request is made to look first, the site is opened and inspected, and the answer says what she will and will not do plus the legal route. The older real-app suites were rewritten for the new layout and pass: `workspace-e2e.mjs` (91 checks), `gemini-keys-e2e.mjs`, `maximize-e2e.mjs`, `recovery-e2e.mjs`.

**Not verified.** No real AI model was run: the checks cover the engine, the tools, the prompt text and the window, not how well Gemini or ChatGPT choose in chat or after the "look first" nudge. When the "Turn on Agent and do it" button resends the words, they appear twice in the conversation (the typed one and the one it sends). A command phrased in an unusual way may still go to the AI in chat, and if the AI says it needs Agent she offers the same button.

## Why "Open MovieRulz site and play Telugu HD Paradise movie" was refused: traced, not guessed (24 Oct 2026)

**How it was traced.** `EYA_TRACE_FILE=<file>` (testing only, `src/main/agent/trace.ts`, wired in `main.ts` and `ipcRouter.ts`) writes every request, the raw words and tool calls of every AI answer, and every tool run with its result, one JSON line each. `tests/live/diagnose-request.mjs` runs requests through the real app with it (`DIAG_REAL=1` copies the real Eya's AI settings into a throwaway profile; nothing is written back).

**What the real model did (Gemini, the exact sentence, Agent on).** `load_tools(browser, system)`, then one `web_search("MovieRulz official site")` (the search returned results, including a MovieRulz address), then, with no other tool call, its own refusal: "I cannot open or play movies from sites like MovieRulz, as they often provide content without permission. I can, however, help you find legal ways to watch the movie…". No site was opened, no film was looked for.
- **The refusal is the model's own words, not code.** No handler produced it: the intent router, chat commands, memory and the fast local layer all passed the sentence on (checked with an AI that only answers "(stand-in)"), and `isBlockedSite` never ran because the browser tool was never called. It is also not a failure to start the browser: the same run opened `example.org` with `open_website` and read it with `read_page` and answered "Example Domain".
- **Why the existing guard did not catch it:** `AgentEngine.ts` (the "refusal before looking" step) only fired when `toolsUsed.length === 0`; the one `web_search` counted as having looked, so the model's refusal was accepted as the final answer. The prompt already says never to refuse from a site's name (`prompts.ts`); this model did anyway, so more prompt text was not the fix.
- **A second dead end behind it:** had the model called `find_and_open_website` with the name only, `navigationRecovery.ts` returned `blocked_site` for a name whose guessed address is on the blocked list (`research/trust.ts` BLOCKED), with "Send me the exact link", having searched nothing.

**The fix (two small changes).** (1) The step that sends a refusal back to look first now treats a `web_search` as *not* looking: it fires unless some other tool (an opened or read page, a file, a film search) was used. (2) For a blocked site given by name only, the tool result now says exactly what is true (it did not open or search for the site, because it gives films away without permission and Eya does not look for or open such sites by name), tells the AI not to claim it searched or checked anything, and names the lawful next step (`deep_research` kind media, or `play_on_youtube`) instead of asking for a link; an exact link from the person is still opened and looked at, with no playback or download. Nothing was added to get around a site's security or limits.

**Verified.** Real model (before the fix): "Hi" in plain chat: chat answer, two tools, no panel, no task; harmless site in Agent mode: `load_tools` → `open_website` → `read_page` → correct answer; the exact sentence with Agent off: the Agent hint, no AI call; with Agent on: the refusal above, traced. After the fix: unit tests (the new engine test fails without the fix and passes with it; the blocked-by-name test), full suite 2727, typecheck, and the real app with a stand-in model replaying the real first answer (`tests/live/agent-mode-e2e.mjs`, 20 checks, case 5b): the refusal is sent back once, `find_and_open_website` runs for the name, the browser does not move, and the tool's words are the new honest ones.

**Round two (the user's report after the first fix).** The real model now relayed the tool's limit ("I can't open MovieRulz. My tools blocked it…") but then ended with "let me know what you're after", although the film was already named: told to look for a legal place itself, it asked instead. So the engine now does that step itself (`AgentEngine.withLegalOptions`, `agentMode.wantedAfterSite`): when `find_and_open_website` answers "blocked site" and the sentence says what was wanted after the site, `deep_research` (kind media, no_site) runs at once for the title with the site name and the opening words removed ("Telugu HD Paradise movie"), and its findings (where it can be watched, free copies) go back to the AI with the blocked answer, with the instruction to tell the user what was found and not to ask what they want. In the real app that took about 6 seconds and found one place to watch and four sources. Opening the site, searching for its address and playing from it are still not done; the limit is deliberate and is said plainly, not hidden behind "an error".

**Not verified.** The real model was not re-run after either fix: its free allowance was used up by the diagnosis runs, so the exact words it writes from the new result are untested (the result it is given was checked in the real app). Asking again when the allowance is back (or with another key) is the one check left.

## Finding a website is Eya's own job; the model interprets (24 Oct 2026, part two)

**The root cause.** Until now, whether a website was found and opened depended on the AI model choosing to call a browser tool. The traced run showed the model calling one web search and then refusing; the refusal was accepted as the answer, the browser was never asked to do anything, and nothing in Eya could say whose "no" it was (the model's, Eya's own rule, a failed search, a page that would not load). A guard that re-asked the model ("look first") tried to talk it round, which is neither reliable nor something to do to a model that has declined.

**The change (architecture, not more prompt).** In Agent mode, when a request plainly names a site, the application does the browser part itself, before the model is asked anything.
- **`src/main/agent/discovery.ts`.** `planDiscovery` reads the request: "open/visit/go to <address> …" (an address, a full link, a local link with a port, "the website example.org") or "open <name> site/website/portal …"; anything less plain (an app, a folder, "my site", no site word) is left to the model. `discoverSite` then runs the existing, tested `find_and_open_website` itself (look up the address if it is a name, open it in Eya's browser, retry a hiccup, check the page really loaded and is the site, follow redirects), and gives the model what really happened, added to the person's own message: the page (address, title, text) or exactly where it stopped, and what is left of the request. The model interprets that and decides what comes next (read, click, fill, answer); it can still use every browser tool.
- **Independent of the model.** Because the step runs first, a site is found and opened even when the AI is out: the person is told "I opened <page>" and then why the AI could not go on (tested with Gemini answering "daily quota" in the real app). It is only for Agent mode, never for chat, never for always-on listening, never for a task being carried on.
- **Whose failure it was (`FailureSource`).** Every failure is attributed and recorded (log, trace, and `AgentResult.diagnosis`): `model_refusal` (the model, or its provider's safety system, declined), `model_unavailable` (no AI could answer), `application_policy` (Eya's own rule about a site), `search` (finding the address failed or found nothing usable), `navigation` (the page did not open, is the wrong site, hit a limit, or needs the person), `tool` (any other). The model is told which, so it reports the right thing. When the answer is itself a "no" from the model and nothing failed before it, the reply says so in one sentence ("the page itself did open (…); the 'no' came from the AI model, not from the browser or the search"), so nobody blames the browser for the model or the model for the browser.
- **Providers report their own declining.** `AICompletion.blocked`: Gemini's blocked prompt or an answer cut by a safety filter, and an OpenAI-style model's stated refusal or content filter, arrive marked as the model's decision instead of an empty answer.
- **No working around a model that declines.** The "send it back to look first" step is gone. A refusal on policy or safety grounds is final: not re-asked, no other provider asked, no new wording. Only a "no" for lack of a tool (the abilities were switched off) is asked again once with everything on, as before.
- **Eya's own rule is the application's, not the model's.** A site that gives films away without permission, named without a link, is not opened or searched for (the step says so as Eya's rule and looks up the lawful options for the film at once, as before). Closed a hole found in the live run: the rule matched only exact names, so a mistyped name ("movieruz", typed into the test window) was searched for and opened a look-alike site. Names within one letter of a listed brand now count (`trust.ts` `isBlockedName`, seven letters or more; ordinary names like "National Library" are untouched).

**Verified.** Typecheck; unit suite 2756 tests (new `tests/discovery.test.ts`, 17: planning, refusal kinds, failure sources, attribution, provider blocks, names; engine tests for each path: page shown to the model, refusal labelled and not argued with, AI out, navigation vs search, blocked site with lawful options, chat and always-on never start it, provider safety block); in the real app with a stand-in model (`agent-mode-e2e.mjs`, 22 checks): an exact link is opened by the application with the model making no tool call, the exact MovieRulz sentence is not opened and the lawful options reach the model, and with the AI unavailable the page is still opened and the person told; `recovery-e2e` 19, `workspace-e2e` 91, `gemini-keys-e2e`, `maximize-e2e` all pass. With the real AI chain (a later provider answered because Gemini's allowance was spent): "Open the website example.org and tell me the main heading" was opened by the application and the model answered from the real page.

**Not verified.** The exact MovieRulz sentence was not run against a real model after this change (Gemini's allowance was spent and the live run was interrupted by a request typed into the test window); what a real model writes from the application's note is untested. The "no" attribution is decided from the opening sentence of the answer, so an unusual refusal wording can be missed (the failure is then simply not labelled). A site that is not on the list and does not look like a listed name is still opened if the search finds it; content-based detection of such sites is not built.

## How many tokens each request takes (24 Oct 2026)

**What it does.** Every request (typed or spoken, chat or Agent) now has its own token count: the sum of every AI answer that came back while Eya worked on it, the AI's reading of an attached file and the web searches that go through the AI included.
- **Where to see it.** Under each Eya answer in the chat, next to "Eya": for example "· 6,300 tokens" (hover for "6,000 tokens sent + 300 in the answer, over 3 AI answers"). In the log, one line per request, `tokens for request`, with calls, prompt, completion, total, tokens by provider (`gemini`, `openai`, `ollama`…) and the total since Eya started. On `AgentResult.tokens` (`{ calls, prompt, completion, total }`). In the trace file when `EYA_TRACE_FILE` is set (event `tokens`; `tests/live/diagnose-request.mjs` prints it).
- **How.** `src/main/providers/ai/tokenMeter.ts`: each provider reports what an answer used the moment it arrives (`recordTokens`: Gemini's `usageMetadata`, an OpenAI-style service's `usage`, Ollama's `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count`); the request's own async context (Node `AsyncLocalStorage`) adds it to that request's count, so nothing is passed through the tools and two requests at the same time never mix. A spoken request is counted once, the transcribing included. "Answer" always includes the thinking the AI did (the total minus the prompt), so sent + answer = total.
- **A request that needed no AI** (a command done locally, the "this is for Agent mode" hint) has no count.

**Not counted.** Spoken replies (Gemini's or ChatGPT's voice), the Gemini Live conversation, and the local speech recognition (those do not report tokens). The since-start total is kept in memory only: it starts again at 0 when Eya restarts, and the per-chat numbers are not kept when a chat is saved and reopened. The number is what the provider reported, not Eya's own estimate.

**Verified.** Unit tests (`tests/tokenMeter.test.ts`: the meter, two requests at once, a spoken request counted once, Gemini/OpenAI/Ollama reporting, the engine's total for a chat answer and for an agent request that reads a file); and in the real app (`agent-mode-e2e.mjs`, 24 checks): a "Hi" shows "128 tokens" (120 + 8, one AI answer), a task that took two AI answers shows 256 over 2, and the log has the `tokens for request` lines. Not verified against the real services' own numbers (the stand-in Google reported the usage in the real app).

## Speed, tokens and not losing work (24 Oct 2026, performance round)

**How it was measured.** `tests/live/measure-requests.mjs` runs fixed requests through the real app with an instant stand-in AI (so the AI's own speed is not in the numbers) and records the time to the answer, AI calls, and the size of the instructions, tool descriptions and messages sent. `tests/workload/thirtyTasks.test.ts` is a repeatable 30-task workload through the real engine, task keeper, record of done calls, provider chain and key pool (the Gemini, ChatGPT-style and Ollama provider classes talk over HTTP to stand-in services that can rate-limit, run out for the day, answer with garbage or be unreachable; the files are real, in a throwaway folder; the web is a modelled set of pages with injected slowness and failures). A task counts only when its outcome is checked (a file on disk, a page state, a record kept, a call count). The same workload was run against the source as it was before this round, for the "before" column.

**What was found (and fixed).**
- *The text waited for the voice.* `AgentEngine.finish` waited for speech to end before returning, so a typed answer arrived as late as it took to be read aloud (923 ms for four words; seconds for a paragraph). A typed request now gets its words back at once and the voice goes on meanwhile (the state goes idle when it ends); a spoken request still waits, so the listening never starts over her own voice. A voice that fails does not break the answer.
- *"Open X" cost a 20,000-token AI call to say "opened".* When a request is only to open a site and the application has opened and checked it (the step added last round), the answer is what it did: no AI call (`doneByEya`).
- *Prompt and tool size.* The browser part of the instructions carried the rules for adverts/YouTube and for ordering/booking on every website request: they are now sent only when the request is about them (prompt topics `ads` and `orders`, `prompts.ts` / `toolGroups.routeTopics`; `load_tools` switches them on, and playing a video does). The rules for working in the browser itself are never held back. The 28 Word tools were sent whenever a request said "heading", "font" or "index" even for a web page: those weak words now count only when the request is not about a website (`toolGroups.WEAK_WORD`).
- *Every step resent every page.* A page result from two steps ago (up to 12,000 characters) is cut down to where it was and what it was called before the next model step (`agent/history.ts` `shortenOlderResults`); only page tools are cut, never a list of files or a path.
- *Reads ran one after another.* When the model asks for several reads that need no page and do not depend on one another (`analyze_document`, `read_file`, `find_file`…) in one turn, up to four run together (`history.canRunTogether`, `startTogether`); everything that touches a page or changes something stays in order. `web_search` is deliberately not in the list (it shares the lookup browser).
- *A retry could do things twice.* What changed something and worked (`tasks/ledger.ts` `SIDE_EFFECT_TOOLS`: files moved, folders made, downloads, settings, memories, power actions) is kept as exact calls with the task, written as it happens (`TaskCompleter.checkpoint`, `TaskStore` `done`), also across Eya being closed and started again. A task that is carried on does not make a call it already made: it is answered "already done" (`AgentEngine`, `req.resume.done`). Used only when a task is carried on, so asking again as a new request still does it.
- *Cancelling a task did not stop it.* A cancelled task went on through its remaining steps (and tokens). The engine now asks between steps (and between the calls of one turn) whether the person stopped it (`shouldStop` ← `TaskCompleter.wasStopped`), and ends with "Okay, I stopped.".
- *Everything out was asked again on every request.* When every AI is out of allowance or credit (a local AI that is not running counts as out), the next request within 30 seconds is told at once, without asking each again (`ChainedAIProvider` `ALL_OUT_PROBE_MS`); they are looked at again every 30 seconds, so added credit is noticed.
- *Whole-file rewrites.* The chat history (rewritten on every message; 1 ms small, 8 ms medium, 39 ms at its largest, measured) is written once for a burst of changes and flushed when the app closes; task progress is written a quarter second later (`storage/CoalescedWriter.ts`). Task state changes are still written at once.

**Results (same tests, before the round and after).** Thirty-task workload: **22 of 30 completed → 30 of 30**; duplicated actions **4 → 0**; failures before were: a cancelled task still made all three folders, a retry repeated create/copy (and again after a restart), the second request asked all providers again (5 requests → 0), three reads took 948 ms → 346 ms, the text waited 923 ms for the voice → 15 ms, and "open a website" used an AI call → none. Input tokens sent: 445,040 → 386,953 (**−13.1%**; −8.3% on the 22 tasks that behave the same in both, the rest comes from calls that are no longer made); AI requests 99 → 89. p95 latency 923 → 346 ms (the median, 31 vs 49 ms, is within the run-to-run noise: the stand-in AI is instant, so these are Eya's own overhead plus the injected delays). Real app, real tools, same requests: a plain "Hi" 2,231 → 331 ms and a question 3,223 → 152 ms to the answer (the voice no longer in the way); a browser request 21,662 → 16,224 tokens per AI call (**−25%**); everything else unchanged within ±1% (Word 10,949 → 10,961; files 9,048 → 9,061).

**Not done / still slow.** (1) A browser request still carries about 16,000 tokens per AI call: the browser rules (about 19,000 characters) and the browser/core tool descriptions (about 31,000) are needed for correct tool use, and I would not cut them without a real model to test against. (2) The real AI's own speed and the free allowances are outside the code. (3) A slow or dead site costs up to 75 s of the application's own opening step (20 s per navigation, one more try after a timeout, up to six pages): measured live with three High Court sites timing out. (4) The memory database is synchronous (`node-sqlite3-wasm`) and was not changed. (5) The 30-task workload models the web and the AI; the real browser and real AI services were exercised by the other live suites, not by this workload. (6) Eya's answer after being sent a 20,000-token browser prompt was not re-measured on a real model (Gemini's allowance was spent).

**Rollback.** A copy of `src` and `tests` as they were before this round is in `C:\Users\Rakesh\Pictures\Advance Eya - backup before perf work (24 Oct 2026)`. Restore with `robocopy "<that folder>\src" "<project>\src" /MIR` (and `tests`), then `npx electron-vite build`. Nothing was committed.

## Stop means stop (24 Oct 2026)

**What was wrong.** The chat box had no Stop button at all (a spinner on a disabled Send), and the Stop buttons that existed only stopped the *next* step: the Browser page's and the panel's Stop blocked the next browser action; the task's Stop only set a flag that the engine looked at between steps. Eya was usually in the middle of an AI answer (seconds, up to a minute), a page load (up to 75 s) or a slow tool, none of which was interrupted, so the chat box stayed on "working" until that ended. Pause was the same: it held only browser steps, not AI answers or other tools.

**What it does now.**
- **A Stop button in the chat box**, a red square where Send was, whenever Eya is working. Pressing it ends the request in that moment: the chat box is released, "Okay, I stopped." is shown, nothing is spoken, the voice is silenced, and nothing more of that request reaches the screen. While she is reading the answer aloud, Send stays usable and a small button beside the microphone silences her.
- **One stop for every Stop button** (the chat box, the Browser page, the panel, a task, "Stop all"): each request has an abort signal (`AgentEngine.stopRequest`, `stopAll`, `supervised`). The request to the AI is cancelled (the connection is closed; no other model, key or provider is asked and none is rested for it, since a Stop is not a failure), a tool that is running is no longer waited for, the application's own search for a site stops, a page that is loading stops loading, and nothing new is begun. A tool already half-way (a file being copied) finishes by itself; its result is ignored. If the engine is stuck in something that cannot be cancelled (the quick local-command layer), the person still gets the answer at once, and the request does nothing more when that ends.
- **Pause holds the whole request**, not only the browser: no AI step and no tool is begun while it is paused (also in plain chat), and Stop works while it is paused.
- **A stopped task is stopped, not failed**: it ends as "Stopped", is not tried again and gives no failure notice; a task that is stopped ends the request behind it at once.
- The chat box itself releases within 1.2 s of Stop even if nothing came back (the main process being busy).

**Verified.** Unit tests (`tests/stopNow.test.ts`, 18: Stop during an AI answer, a tool, the site search, plain chat, an uncancellable local layer, a pause; the right request only; the voice; nothing remembered; task stopped not failed; Gemini/key pool/chain/ChatGPT-style/Ollama requests really cancelled without resting anything; one test fails if the immediate return is removed). Real app (`tests/live/stop-e2e.mjs`, 16 checks, stand-in AI that takes 25 to 40 s and a page that never answers): the Stop button appears; Stop with the AI slow releases the chat box in about a second, the AI request is seen cancelled, no late answer arrives; Stop with a page loading ends it at once and the AI is never asked afterwards; Pause holds the AI step until Resume, and Stop works while paused; the Browser page's Stop and a task's Stop end the request at once, the task shown as stopped; a new request afterwards works; while she speaks Send stays and the small button silences her. The older suites pass (agent-mode 24, workspace 91, recovery 19; see the notes for embedded/keys/maximize).

**Not verified / limits.** Whether the real voice really goes quiet at once was not heard (the call that stops it was); a Stop cannot take back what a tool had already done; an AI request that the service had already started is cancelled on our side but the service may still count it against the allowance; the Home panel's own Pause/Stop buttons are the same calls as the Browser page's, tested through those calls rather than by clicking each.

## Why one request cost 200,000 tokens, and what was done about it (24 Oct 2026, token investigation)

**What was reported.** "Find the Monday cause list ... Telangana High Court" took 5+ minutes and about 200,000 tokens; "Open YouTube and play a feel-good Telugu song" about 80,000. Both numbers are real, not a metering error: the figure under the answer is what the AI services reported for the request (prompt + answer, summed over every call; nothing is estimated and nothing is counted twice: 16 usage reports for 16 calls in the traced run, and the app's total equalled their sum to the token). What it does **not** include is a call that was sent and then timed out or was refused (the provider may still have counted it; the trace now logs those with their time).

**Where the tokens went (traced on the real app and the real AI chain, `EYA_TRACE_FILE`, `tests/live/analyze-trace.mjs`).** Every model step resends the same fixed block before any conversation: the instructions (30,716 characters) and the schemas of 38 tools (31,110), about **15,500 tokens a step**. A court-site request ran 16 steps (the limit) and cost **268,648 tokens in 74 s**, ending with "That took more steps than I can do in one go": 16 calls x about 16,800. The model wandered: it re-opened the same page three times, repeated the same click and the same date twice, sent `go_back` an argument it does not have (refused: a whole step), and had one click refused because a drop-down menu was drawn over the link, and never reached the portal that has the advocate-wise list. The YouTube request made 2 to 5 model calls to arrive at one tool call (`play_on_youtube`). In your real Eya the same request was cut at the 180 s browser budget twice (203 s and 298 s in `chats.json`), and the kept task then ran the whole loop again (`tasks.json`: `attempts: 2`; the limit was 8 runs).

**Why so many steps and so slow (confirmed in the provider log).**
- Gemini 3 models answered HTTP **400 "Function call is missing a thought_signature"** to the second step of every request whose first step had been answered by another AI (the call that AI made has no Gemini signature). Both models that were working (`gemini-3-flash-preview`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview`) refused every later step, so each step went on to the free models of other services (about 16 s a step, rate-limited, poor at tools). Checked against Google directly: without the signature both models return that 400; with Google's documented stand-in value (`skip_thought_signature_validator`) both answer. Fixed (`GeminiAIProvider.withForeignCallSignatures`).
