- "Fail fast" tried only the **first two** Gemini models before handing the request to another service, so when those two were the slow or spent ones (3.8 under high demand, 3.6 out for the day) the two that work were never reached. It now goes past models that fail at once (a quota, a refusal, high demand: about a second each) and stops only when the round has used 8 s (a slow model), up to four models.
- A prompt of 17,000 tokens a step also uses the free per-minute allowance about twice as fast as a prompt of half that size, which is what puts every Gemini key to rest.

**What was changed.**
- **No model at all for "open YouTube and play <song>"** (`local/localIntents.ts` `play`, `LocalCommands.playOnYoutube`): recognised by its shape (YouTube must be named: a bare "play <name>" is still a file on the PC), one call to `play_on_youtube`, and what is said is what the tool reported ("Playing ...", or exactly why it is only open). A film with no official upload, or a failure that is not about the browser, still goes to the model. Not done from always-on listening. Eya Browser could neither skip adverts nor tell whether a song had started (the adverts helper existed only for the user's own Chrome through the extension): `EmbeddedPageHost` now runs the same page-script command, so it does both.
- **A smaller fixed block, never at the cost of a rule.** The two browser sections were rewritten without repetition (21,300 to 18,000 characters; every rule kept, checked by a test that lists the safety and navigation rules); the YouTube player tools are sent only for requests about a song, film or video (`TOOL_TOPIC`); and once a request has begun working in the browser and stayed in the web, the tools and instructions about the PC itself (other applications, windows, files and folders, the clipboard) are set aside for the next steps (`toolGroups.browseFocus`). The first step is never narrowed, any PC word, any other tool group, any file seen (a finished download), or `load_tools pc` brings them straight back, and "I can't" with abilities off still switches everything on. Result on the real app: **step 0 14,210 tokens; every later step about 11,500** (was 15,500).
- **Fewer wasted steps.** A link that something is drawn over (a menu dropping over another) is followed directly instead of failing (`injected.js`: only links; a button behind a dialog is still refused); a tool call with an argument the tool does not have runs without it and says so, in the agent's step loop only (`ToolRegistry.invoke(..., { lenient: true })`; tools that ask for a yes, the live voice bridge and the quick commands stay strict); a weekday with no date means the next one from today and the date used is said; a table now carries `dataRows`, the rows counted by the page script (it showed only six and a header-inclusive total), and the model is told to give that number.
- **No endless re-running.** A run cut short by the clock is carried on, but not again when a run got no further than the one before it, and never past the third (`TaskCompleter.settle`): the person is told and what was done is kept. (It was 8 runs of up to 16 steps.)
- **Seeing it.** The trace (`EYA_TRACE_FILE`) now has the time of every AI answer and tool, a `usage` event per call (prompt, answer, total, cached), and `tests/live/analyze-trace.mjs` prints per-call sizes, usage by provider, the tool timeline, repeated calls and where the time went. Cached tokens (Gemini implicit caching, OpenAI) are read and kept apart; the tooltip on the count says how many were cached. `play_on_youtube` logs where its time went (search / open / start).

**Measured (real app, same conditions).**

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| "Open YouTube and play a feel-good Telugu song" (real YouTube, Eya Browser) | 2 AI calls, 32,583 tokens, 50 s, did not play (the extension was not answering) | **0 AI calls, 0 tokens**, 49 s, *Playing 90s Feel good Telugu Love Songs*, playback checked (13 adverts skipped); 1.5 s to find it, 31.7 s for the page to open, 12 s to start |
| fixed block per AI step (court-site request) | 30,716 + 31,110 chars = 15,456 tokens | step 0: 27,502 + 29,182 = 14,171; later steps 23,554 + 21,101 = **11,164 (-28%)** |
| court-site request, scripted AI on a local copy of a court site: 4 AI calls | (same code path) | step 0 14,210, then 11,498 / 11,787 / 12,015 tokens; covered link followed; extra argument set aside; 7 rows counted by the page script; total 49,558 = the sum the AI reported |

**Not verified (read this).** (1) The court-site request was **not re-run with a real model**: today OpenRouter's and OpenAI's allowances are spent (OpenAI has no credit, the DeepSeek key is refused, Ollama is not running) and Gemini returned 503 "high demand" / daily 429 for the first two models, so a real run had no AI to use. The 16-step, 268,648-token run above is the last real measurement; what it would cost now is a projection (about 14,200 + 15 x (11,200 + messages) ≈ 210,000 *if the model again took 16 steps*: about -20%), not a result. How many steps a real model takes is the biggest remaining factor, and with the Gemini fix a stronger model should be the one answering when it has allowance (the signature fix was verified against Google, the model-reach change by unit tests only). (2) Opening YouTube's watch page in Eya Browser took 31 to 58 s in this test profile (the first load once timed out at 20 s and was retried); in your own Chrome (your saved setting is My Browser) it could not be measured because the test profile has no paired extension. (3) The reduction on a request with a real model, a real extension and a real page was not measured. (4) The extension in your Chrome keeps its old page script until you reload it once in `chrome://extensions` (the covered-link click and the row count live there; Eya Browser has them already).

**Tests.** `tests/geminiForeignCalls.test.ts` (written the way Google behaves; fails without the fix), `tests/playOnYoutube.test.ts`, `tests/browseFocus.test.ts` (the rules and the engine; fails without the narrowing), `tests/toolRegistryArgs.test.ts`, `tests/tokenEconomy.test.ts` (size budget, rules still present, YouTube tools only for video requests), additions in `backupAi`, `embeddedPageHost`, `pageState`, `taskCompleter`; live: `tests/live/token-economy-e2e.mjs` (13 checks; the covered-link check was shown to fail with the fix removed). Suites that pass: unit 2,905 passed (167 files), agent-mode, workspace, recovery, stop, embedded, gemini keys, maximize.

## Choosing the right person, and sending to them exactly once (25 Oct 2026)

**What went wrong with "Send this PDF to Anna on WhatsApp"** (found in the code, then reproduced against a stand-in chat page and, for the click and the header, in a real Chrome with the real extension):
- *The chat was opened by the whole text of its row* (name, last message, time, unread count) and that text had to be unchanged between reading the list and the click. A chat row changes whenever a message arrives or the minute turns, so a chat that was found was then "could not be opened" (`find_chat` → `openFound` → `clickOnPage(row text)`).
- *The header check was a substring check and only informational.* "Anna" is in the header "Ramesh Anna", so a wrong chat passed as the right one, and the question named the chat that was *meant*, not the one that was *open*. Nothing stopped an attachment or a Send in the wrong chat.
- *The same search was typed again and again.* Every `find_chat` call retyped the words; the "page just opened and shows nothing yet" loop called it up to 5 more times, and the "sounds like" fallback typed a different search in between, so the list flipped back and forth and never settled.
- *One name that merely contains the words was taken as an identity.* A single partial match ("Ramesh Anna" for "Anna") was opened as if it were exact.
- *The request was not understood before the clicks:* "open WhatsApp and send msg to <number> "hello"" matched nothing; any recipient with 4 or more digits was refused; the quotes stayed in the message; "this PDF" was searched for as a file called "pdf"; "message Anna on snapchat" became a WhatsApp message to someone called "Anna on snapchat".
- *Nothing remembered what had been sent.* After a send whose result was not seen, the same request prepared and could send it again.
- *With WhatsApp for Windows installed, a message to a number went to the installed program* (where Eya cannot type or check) even with a browser connected; and `open_application` called an already-running app a failure after waiting 6 s.

**What it does now**
- **The request becomes a task before anything is clicked** (`chat/chatTask.ts`, `ChatCommands.begin`): app, action, recipient (a name or a number), what is to be sent. States `RESOLVE_RECIPIENT … VERIFY_SENT_RESULT → COMPLETED / FAILED`, entered only on evidence, never back to redo a verified step; each step has a bounded number of retries (2), each on a fresh look; a failure has a category from a short list. The same request again while the earlier one is open is the same task, not a new one. A finished send ends the task: a stray "yes" afterwards sends nothing.
- **How sure Eya is** (`contactMatch.ts`: `evidence`, `verified`). An exact name (case and spacing ignored) or a whole phone number identifies a chat. A name that contains the words, the same words in another order, a close spelling, the end of a number, the chat you message most: a *likely* match, said to be one when you are asked to go ahead ("Found Ramesh Anna (matched only on "Anna", not an exact name). Send …?"). With no exact name and several candidates she asks once ("Which one: Ramesh Anna; Anna Reddy; or Anna Kumar?"), opens nothing, and does not ask again once you answer. What you choose is an identity.
- **Opening the right row** (`BrowserItemClicker.clickItem`, `pageState.findListItem`, `ChromeBrowserService.clickItem`): the row is found again by its first line in the page *as it is when the click happens*, and clicked with its current name. A refused or stale click is looked at again (twice at most by the browser layer, once more by the task), not repeated on the old look.
- **Verifying the conversation.** After the click the header is read (waiting for a slow one), against the *longer names that contain the chosen name*. It is remembered with the chat. Before a file is attached, and before a Send *even with your yes*, the open chat is checked against it: a different chat means nothing is attached or sent and she says so (`status: wrong_chat`). A header that cannot be read is said so, and then there is nothing to compare with.
- **Searching once** (`find_chat`: `searchOnce`): the same words on the same page are typed once for 20 seconds; asking again reads the search that is already there. The sounds-alike look is tried once per name. A chat that is already open and asked for by exactly its name is not searched for or clicked again.
- **Files.** "this PDF" is the file in view (the one just found or opened), and only if it is that kind of file; with nothing in view she asks which. The attachment is checked on the page before Send; a file the page never shows is not sent.
- **Never sent twice on a guess** (`ChatSession.noteSend`, `verify_sent`, `ChatCommands.priorSend`, `attach_file`). A send is recorded as begun *before* Send is pressed. If the result was not seen (no mark, "sending", a timeout) it stays *not known*. The same message or file to the same chat again: the chat is looked at first; if it is there the person is told and nothing is prepared again; "already sent a moment ago" is said when asked. This does not claim exactly-once delivery: when the page gives no evidence she says so and asks.
- **One place for what is particular to an app** (`chat/platformAdapter.ts`): address, search box, message box labels, login words, Send/Attach labels, capabilities. WhatsApp and Telegram have one; Instagram and the rest do not, and a request that names them goes to the model, not to WhatsApp.
- **Numbers.** Any whole phone number is a recipient in every wording; the quotes it was said inside are removed from the message. With a browser connected the web page is used (typed, sent, checked); the installed program only when there is no browser, as before. `open_application` knows an app that is already running (`runningImageOf`) and says so at once.
- **Diagnostics** (`chat/chatDiagnostics.ts`, `%APPDATA%\eya\chat-diagnostics.jsonl`, about 400 newest lines): task id, step, operation, state change, time in ms, ok, attempts, a category, whether the expected result was seen. Never a message, a name, a number or page text (every text field is cut to plain tokens, runs of 7+ digits are removed). Covers the model's own chat steps too.

**Verified.** Unit tests (2,987 pass in 172 files, typecheck clean): `chatRecipientFlow` (35, the Anna scenarios, one search, blocked clicks, slow header, late marks, uncertain sends, duplicates, resumed task, privacy of what is written), `chatTask` (16), `chatRequestParsing` (18), `chatClickItem` (9), `chatSendLedger` (4), and changes to the existing chat suites (one old expectation changed on purpose: a first-name-only match is now said to be one). Six protections were each removed in turn and a test failed every time. **Real Chrome with the real extension** (`tests/live/chatflow.live.test.ts` 22 including 5 new Anna tests, `chatcommands.live.test.ts` 9) against a WhatsApp-style and a Telegram-style *mock* page, now with four Annas: the exact name is opened, the header check tells "Anna" from "Ramesh Anna" in a real DOM, an attach and a Send in the wrong chat are refused, nothing arrives in the page. Real-app suites pass (agent-mode, workspace, token-economy).

**NOT verified: anything on the real WhatsApp Web.** No real message or file was sent, and the real page was not opened by any test. Unknown until it is run with your say-so: whether its search box, rows, header, composer, Send button and delivery marks carry the labels these steps rely on (a header that cannot be read means "I couldn't read the chat's header to double-check it" and no wrong-chat protection); what WhatsApp Web does for a number that is not among your chats (still "no chat found"); two contacts with the identical display name (indistinguishable before opening); whether WhatsApp allows a second web session for any link-based approach. The earlier "your own instruction counts as the yes" question is not changed: she still asks before every send.

## Why "send msg to anna in whatsapp 'where are u'" kept failing, and sending files through "Select chats" (25 Oct 2026, round 2)

**How the real WhatsApp Web page was studied.** With the user's say-so (read-only), Eya's own extension looked at the open WhatsApp tab and wrote only the *structure* — kinds of controls, where they sit, the app's own words, and for everything else (a name, a number, a message) only its *shape* ("Aaaa 99:99") — to `%APPDATA%\eya\page-probe.jsonl` (`chat/pageProbe.ts`, `chat/pageProbeRunner.ts`; switched on only by `EYA_PAGE_PROBE=1`; the extension's read-only `probe` op). What it showed:
- With **no chat open**, the page has one file input that takes anything. A file given to it makes WhatsApp open its own **"Select chats"** popup (a search box "Search name, number or @username", a tick box and a button per person, section titles). A **Send** button appears in the popup once someone is ticked; Send sends and WhatsApp **opens that person's chat**.
- An open chat's message box is called **"Type a message to <name>"** — the page itself says which chat is open. (The header is not a heading, so it could not be read; that is why every "double-check" said it could not.)
- The left search box is called "Search or start a new chat" only while it is empty; with text in it the page leaves the label off and the box is known only by a generated id (`_r_a_`).
- With a chat open the only file input is the message box's (photos); it does not take a PDF.

**Verified causes of the failures in the reproduced conversation**
1. *The first message was not carried out by Eya at all.* `send msg to anna in whatsapp 'where are u'` was read as "name and message, not told apart", and with no saved names `ChatCommands.message` gave up and handed it to the AI — although the quotation marks say where the message is. (`chatIntents.unsplit` now splits at quotation marks, at "name, msg words", and understands "i said send msg in whatsapp to anna, msg …".) *Reproduced by a test that fails before the fix.*
2. *Every failure was said as "I couldn't look for that chat just now".* A privacy switch that is off, a browser not connected, a timeout and a missing tab were one sentence (`readFound`), and `find_chat` kept no kind of error. It now says which (`data.reason`, `browserReasonWords`), and the diagnostics keep it (`statusWord`: `error:timeout`, `browser_unavailable:not_connected`, `communication_access_off`). *The exact error of that one run could not be recovered: Eya's log only goes to the console.*
3. *"Opened" meant "the click was made".* On the real page the click did not open the chat (the pane still showed the home screen and the typed search), `find_chat` said "opened" anyway, and `attach_file` then gave the file to the page's only file input — which opened the Select chats popup that nothing knew how to use. A chat is now open only when the **page shows it** (its message box names the chat); otherwise `not_opened`, retried once from a fresh look, then said plainly (`confirmOpened`, `conversationState`).
4. *A click is sent to the element Eya chose, not to the one a real click would land on.* An app that listens on an inner node of a row (React chat lists) never hears it. The click now lands on the innermost element under the pointer and bubbles up (`injected.js clickOp`). *Verified in a real Chrome with a page built that way: the new test fails without the fix. Not verified: that this is why WhatsApp ignored the click.*
5. *The names inside the Select chats popup were counted as the chat's header* (`headerMentions` read open dialogs): "already open" was said at 35 ms with no chat open. The popup is now never taken for a header, and a popup left open is closed before searching.
6. *The search box was no longer recognised once text was typed in it* ("still loading" for ever, no second search possible). WhatsApp's adapter now knows the generated id; an open chat's message box also means "ready".
7. *Groq's Llama wrote its tool calls as text* (`{"name":"clickonlink","parameters":{…}}`); `OpenAICompatibleProvider` returned that as the answer, and `AgentEngine` shows an answer with no tool call as the result. `agent/textToolCalls.ts` now reads such an answer: a call to a **registered** tool (exact name, or the same name without underscores) is run as the real call through the same dispatcher and gates; one that cannot be run (`clickonlink` is not a tool; JSON cut short; one bad call among good ones) is **never shown and never run** — the model is told, twice at most, then the person is told honestly that nothing was done. A name is never guessed from a similar one, and JSON inside prose is prose.
8. *A phone number was made up* ("+1 800 234 5678"). Nothing checked where a number in an answer came from. `agent/factGuard.ts`: a number in an answer must be in what was said, in an earlier line, or in a tool's result; any other is taken out and the answer says Eya has no verified number. Prompt rules say the same.
9. *"ok", "do it now", "go and do what I said", "send na" were not understood.* "send na" was not even a yes, and a request that failed left nothing behind, so the next short message was a new request with no recipient and no words. `ChatCommands.retry` keeps the request that did not finish; `isContinuation` says which short messages mean "go on with it". It lives in `ChatCommands`, not in an AI's conversation, so it is the same whichever AI answers — or none (a test runs it with every AI out of credit). Once the request went, "ok" is not "send it again".
10. *Every send asked again* although the person had said to send it. `confirmSends` (default `'unless_verified'`): the request is the instruction, and when the **page** has shown that the open chat is the person's (an exact name or a whole number, named by the message box) it is sent; a likely match, a chat the page did not confirm, and a send like one begun earlier are asked about first. `'always'` is the old behaviour.
11. *"Sent" was said whatever the page showed.* The mark the page showed is what is said: sent ("not yet delivered"), delivered, read. No mark is "in the chat, can't confirm". Only a mark that is new counts, and an uncertain send is looked at in the chat before anything is sent again (`priorSend`, `verify_sent`).

**Files go through "Select chats"** (`share_file_to_chat`, `ChatCommands.shareFile`; the old open-the-chat way stays for apps without that step and as `fileRoute: 'chat'`). Home screen first (a popup is closed; an open chat is closed with **Escape** — the extension's `press_key`, Escape only — and, if that does not close it, the page is loaded again), the file is given to the page once, the name or number is typed **in the popup's own search box**, the person is ticked and the page is asked whether *that person alone* is ticked (an out-of-sight tick is noticed by the popup's Send), Send is pressed, and the chat WhatsApp opens is checked for the file and its mark. Several fit: one question. Nobody fits: said, the popup closed, nothing invented. The left search box and the chat list are not used at all.

**Tests.** `tests/messagingRegression.test.ts` (35: the 12 cases of the report, plus files), `tests/chatPickerRoute.test.ts` (22), `tests/textToolCalls.test.ts` (11), `tests/pageProbe.test.ts` (5), against `tests/chatPickerFixtures.ts` — a mock built from what the probe showed. Real Chrome with the real extension: a click on an inner-listener row (fails without the fix) and Escape (`tests/live/chromeExtension.live.test.ts`).

**NOT verified on the real WhatsApp Web:** that the innermost-element click opens a chat row; that Escape closes a chat; the file input at the home screen with a different WhatsApp build; what the popup does for a number that is not among the chats; the delivery marks right after Send (the chat is opened a moment after, so a very fast send may show a mark that was already there and be called "can't confirm"); two contacts with the same display name. **The extension in the browser must be reloaded once** for the click, Escape and probe changes.

## Reliable automatic browser control — step 1 (26 Oct 2026)

**Goal.** "Open <site> and do <thing>" works through Eya's own authorised browser access, with no screen sharing, and every step is judged from the page that comes back.

**The path, and what was wrong on it** (traced in the code, then measured in a real Chrome with the real extension, `tests/live`):
instruction → `planDiscovery` / `find_and_open_website` (the app opens and checks the page before any AI call) → `AgentEngine` tool loop → `BrowserSessionManager` (which browser) → `ChromeBrowserService` → the extension (`open_url`, `observe`, `click`) → the page state that comes back → the answer.

| Root cause (verified) | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| **Every click and every navigation waited 2.2 s to see whether a download started.** | Measured: a click took 2.8–3.1 s, reading the page 7 ms. | The wait is for a click or address that *looks like* it fetches a file (a download link, a control that says "Download", an address ending .pdf); any other action only looks. A file that begins later is reported by the **next** action, once (`tabs.js awaitDownload`, `actions.js runAction`, `injected.js clickOp`). **Now ~0.9–1.1 s per click, ~1.2 s per open.** |
| **A failed page was accepted as "opened".** The extension never told Eya what the site answered (no `webRequest` permission, and no `navigation` in the reply), so a 503/404 page counted as the page asked for. The desktop side already knew what to do with a status — it was never given one. | Live: `/r/flaky` (503 once) came back as `ok`. | The extension reads the answer from the browser's own navigation record (`performance … responseStatus`, no new permission) and sends it (`navInfo`, `open_url`). A 503 is retried, a 404 is said to be missing. |
| **A retry did not retry.** When an address was already showing in a tab, `openUrl` only brought the tab forward ("left as it was"), so the retry after a failure saw the same error. | Live: the flaky page never recovered. | A try after a failure loads that tab again (`fresh`: `NavigationRecovery` → `openWebsite` → `open_url` → `tabs.reload`). |
| **A dead address was reported as "opened as a blank page"**, and a different port on the same machine counted as "the same site". | Live: `127.0.0.1:59999` → "opened as a blank page". | The browser's own "can't be reached" page is recognised and said so (`could not be reached`); tabs are told apart by port. |
| **"I can't interact with your browser — please share your screen."** Nothing told the model it had hands in the browser (only which tabs were open, and nothing at all when none was connected); the refusal check covered only "no tool / can't browse", and only while abilities were switched off. | Code: `browserWorld.summary()`, `refusalOf`, `routeGroups`. | (1) The model is told, in every request about a website, exactly what it can do and that it is already allowed — or exactly what is missing and what to do, never "share your screen" (`browser/browserAccess.ts`, wired as `browserAccess`). (2) A prompt rule. (3) The engine answers a false "I can't use the browser / share your screen" with what is true, up to twice, and switches the browser abilities on if they were off (`agent/browserClaims.ts`); not when a browser step really reported that no browser is there, and not for a request that is about the screen itself. |
| **"Clicked" was reported whatever happened.** | Code: `succeeded()`. | A click after which the page is exactly as it was is said to have changed nothing (`nothingChanged`), and the model is not told it worked. |
| **Controls were found by exact words only.** | Code: `findBestTextMatchIndex`. | After the existing exact/contains matching, a control is found by what it means: the same letters (`cause list` / `CauseList`), all the words in any order, the same purpose (`sign in` / `Log in`, `basket` / `Cart`), or an address that says it (`/causelist`) — each only when it points at exactly one control. |
| **An AI call (and its tokens) for the plainest request.** | `planDiscovery` kept "…and click X" for the model. | When what is left after opening is one plain click, Eya makes it herself with the same `click_on_page` tool (every check on a click still applies) and answers from the page that comes back — **no AI call** — only if the page showed a change; anything else goes to the model with what happened (`discovery.planClick`, `describeClick`). |

**How to test it by hand** (needs the extension reloaded once: `chrome://extensions` → Eya Browser Bridge → reload):
1. Make sure the badge on the extension icon says ON (or ask Eya "is my browser connected?" — `browser_status`).
2. Say: **"Open example.org and click More information"** (or any site and a link on it). Eya opens it and clicks; it should take a couple of seconds and answer "I clicked … and the page changed. It now shows …". No screen sharing at any point.
3. Say: **"Open <a site> and click <a control that does nothing>"**: it must NOT say it worked.
4. Say: **"Open http://127.0.0.1:59999/"**: it must say it could not be reached, not that it opened.
5. Ask her to click a link by a different wording (`log in` for a "Sign in" link): it should still find it.

**Tests.** Unit: `tests/controlMeaning.test.ts` (42: meaning match, access statement, claim detection, `planClick`), `tests/browserControlEngine.test.ts` (12: the engine with a scripted AI that claims it cannot use the browser, the cases where it must not be argued with, the open-then-click path with no AI call). Real Chrome with the real extension and real tools: `tests/live/browserControl.live.test.ts` (8: false "share your screen" corrected and the real page read, open-and-click with no AI call, a control found by meaning, a click that changes nothing, a click is ~1 s, a download link still waited for, a late download reported by the next action, a 503 recovered / a 404 and a dead address said not to have opened). Each fix was taken out in turn and a test failed every time.

**Not covered / limits.** The real sites and real sign-ins (the test profile is empty); a real model (a scripted one stands in, so what is proved is Eya's own code — the nudge cannot make a model obey, only give it a second chance with the true facts); the HTTP status of a page reached by a *click* (only an address Eya opens reports one); the browser's own error page cannot be told apart by cause (refused / DNS / timeout) because that needs a `webNavigation` permission the extension does not ask for.

## Phone numbers and "who do you mean" — natural recipient resolution (27 Oct 2026)

**Goal.** Say who a message or file is for the way people really say it — `+91 9361113900`, `9361113900`, `93611-13900`, "the number ending 3900", the digits read out ("nine three six triple one…"), "him", "the person I messaged yesterday" — and have Eya send only when she knows who it is, keep the message exactly, and never send twice.

**What was wrong (verified in the code).** (1) Any 10 digits were given India's `+91`, valid or not — so a number that fits nowhere became someone else's. (2) "the last 4 digits 3900" typed `43900` (the 4 was read as a digit). (3) Digits read out, or in Telugu/Hindi script, were not numbers at all. (4) The end of a number and a whole number went through the same code; the app's own top-bar buttons ("Meta AI", "Go to Calls", "Invite a friend") could be offered as people for a number. (5) "him" / "the same person" went to the AI with no history. (6) Collapsing double spaces in a message when "on WhatsApp" was in the request.

**What was built.**
- `chat/phoneNumbers.ts` — reads a number with **libphonenumber-js** (`/max`, MIT, ~154 KB of rules): a country code is added **only** when the number is valid in the configured country; otherwise the digits stay exactly as given (`unclear`). Understands spaces, hyphens, brackets, trunk `0`, `00`, a spoken `plus`, digits said aloud (`double`, `triple`), other scripts, "ending/starting/last N digits". `maskNumber` shows `+91 ••••••3900`.
- `chat/recipientResolver.ts` — the one place that decides **who**, with no WhatsApp in it: `resolveRecipient(what was said, rows, options)` returns `status` (resolved / confirm / ambiguous / not_found / unreadable), `confidence`, `candidates` with the evidence for each, `normalized` number, `needsClarification`, one focused `question` (numbers masked), `safeToSend`. Order: whole number on the chat itself → the app's own search for the whole number → the end of a number (only an identity when the chat itself shows a number ending in ≥6 matching digits and nobody else fits) → name → the conversation. Reusable for any chat app.
- Settings → General → **Country of my phone numbers** (India by default; a made-up code is refused). Numbers said *with* a country code are always read as written.
- `find_chat` and `share_file_to_chat` use the resolver for numbers; the number is searched as its national digits and, if nobody shows, once more with the country code; the app's own buttons are never candidates for a number (stricter row filter + more control names).
- "him / her / the last person I messaged / the same person I messaged yesterday" → `ChatSession.recentRecipients()` (in memory, names and times) and `ContactBook.recentUses()` (the names the person let Eya keep, with when). One person is **said back for a yes**; several are asked about; nobody known → "him" is left to the AI (it has the whole talk), the others get one focused question. A pronoun, a phrase or digits are never kept as a name for a chat.
- Candidate lists never read a whole number out: a chat listed under its number is shown masked.

**Choices worth knowing.** A direct "send X to <whole number>" is the instruction to send once the page identifies the chat. The end of a number, the same digits under a different country code, a landline-style number read from your country, and anything by "him" are said back and need a yes — they are not identities. Same digits under another country code are only asked about when the code was *not* said; `+91 …` never matches a `+1 …` chat.

**Tests.** `tests/phoneNumbers.test.ts` (38), `tests/recipientResolver.test.ts` (54), `tests/recipientRouting.test.ts` (55: the real parser, ChatCommands, chat tools and resolver against the mock chat pages — whole/national/spaced/spoken/Telugu numbers, endings, duplicate endings, missing numbers, invalid numbers, another country, "him", "yesterday", files through "Select chats", exact message kept, a repeat is not sent, aliases never kept), settings (1 new). Ten safety rules were each broken in turn and a test failed every time. Real Chrome: `tests/live/chatcommands` (9) and `chatflow` (22) pass. Real app: `node tests/live/country-setting-e2e.mjs` (8: Settings row, change, file, refusal of a made-up country, restart).

**Not verified.** Real WhatsApp Web (nothing was sent to or read from it): the mocks encode what the 25 Oct probe saw — in particular that the app's search finds a saved contact by its national digits (and by the country-code form when it does not) is **assumed**, as is that a chat list row shows a number only for unsaved contacts. The installer was not rebuilt (the library is a production dependency, so it is included by electron-builder; size not measured). Gender/relationship words ("my brother") are names, handled by aliases as before.

## Plugins: one way to add a service, WhatsApp first (27 Oct 2026)

**What it is.** A plugin is a *manifest* (what it is, what it needs, what it can do, how each result is checked) plus a *connector* (Eya's own code that says whether the account is linked). Its actions are carried out by tools in the **one** `ToolRegistry` the agent, the quick commands and the live voice already use — no second registry, loop, task store or browser bridge. No code is downloaded: a manifest can only name tools that already exist (checked when it is loaded), and only plugins that ship with Eya can be installed. A catalogue source is an interface (`CatalogSource`) so an online one could be added later; today the page says plainly that none is connected.

**Four separate steps, each the person's own** (Plugins page, in the sidebar): **installed** → **switched on** → **connected** (their own account) → **allowed** (one permission at a time). Installing connects and allows nothing. Connecting and granting are reachable only from that page (IPC from the app's own window; no tool, voice command or web page). WhatsApp's "connect" *is* Communication Access — the same switch, not a second authority — and connecting it never allows another chat app. Uninstalling takes the permissions and the account link away and is not undone by itself; an update never gives itself a new permission.

**How it is enforced.** `ToolRegistry.setGuard`: a plugin's tool that cannot be used (not installed / off / not connected / permission not granted) is **hidden from the AI** and **refused if called**, with a reason for the person and a note to the AI that it is theirs to change. The tools a plugin shares with everything else (typing in a box, clicking Send) can't be held by name, so the **Send gate** that already guards every Send in a chat app also asks the plugin: a message needs "send text messages", a file "send files", and a person's *yes* cannot give a permission. A message is refused before it is typed. One line in the AI's context says which plugins it can use.

**WhatsApp's actions, as built.** Find a chat (name, part of a name, number, end of a number — see the recipient resolver), remember chat names, send a text message, send a file, check a send (WhatsApp's own marks). **Declared not supported, with no tool and no permission:** find messages, save a file received in a chat — nothing is promised that is not there.

**Tasks that survive.** Each WhatsApp request is kept as a task through the existing `TaskCompleter` (not a duplicate): plugin, action, phase (received → planning → executing → verifying → completed; or waiting for an answer / a permission, failed, cancelled, **uncertain**), who it settled on and on what evidence (names only; a number is masked), and what was *seen* (typed, Send pressed, the chat showed it as sent). The Send being pressed is written before the check that follows it. A plugin task is **never carried on by itself** and cannot be resumed from the list: if Eya closes mid-send it comes back as uncertain ("may already have gone — look in the chat"), and asking again makes Eya look in the chat first. A fingerprint (hash; no name, no word) of each recent send is kept across a restart (`send-journal.json`), so a repeat after a restart is looked at, not sent twice.

**First run.** A bundled plugin is installed and switched on; what was already allowed carries over (you had allowed WhatsApp → it is connected with its three abilities), otherwise nothing is allowed.

**Tests.** `tests/plugins.test.ts` (36: manifests and validation, catalogue, lifecycle, gating, the registry guard, the Send gate, the connector), `tests/pluginPlatform.test.ts` (29: the whole slice against the mock chat page — task phases and evidence, no message or whole number on disk, questions, not-allowed cases, uncertain sends, restart, a cut-off task, the engine using the tools of two plugins in one request). 18 safety rules were each broken in turn and a test failed every time. Real app (built, isolated profile): `node tests/live/plugins-e2e.mjs` (22 checks: the page, the four steps, the real registry refusing a tool, restart, uninstall).

**What the real WhatsApp Web showed (27 Oct 2026, read-only, in the user's own signed-in session via the real extension).** (1) Plugin ready, Edge connected, WhatsApp Web signed in. (2) **A real bug the mocks could not show:** a search for "You" took the **"You" button of WhatsApp Web's left rail (the profile)** for a chat and clicked it; the page opened the profile/Settings view instead, no message box came up, and Eya said so and did nothing — the post-click check held. Fixed: a button with no row text of its own is never a chat (`toCandidates`), regression test added (fails without the fix), and on the real page the same search now says "no chat found". (3) Chats list rows, the New chat panel and its "Message yourself" row were seen and match what the code expects. (4) The user's self-chat is listed under their username with "(You)" (not under their name), which is why searching "You" or their name found nothing.
**Second real-page finding (same day, from the user's own request "send hi to uber chat"):** Eya refused with "something went wrong in the browser". Two causes. (1) WhatsApp Web labels the message box of this chat with its PHONE NUMBER ("Type a message to +1 (940) 290-7669"), not the saved name "Uber"; the check "does the box name the chat I clicked?" took that for a different chat, although the page's header showed a button named "Uber". Fixed: when the box names a number, the header's button named exactly like the chat settles it (`headerNamesChat`); the box must go on naming that same number (`OpenChat.composerAs`, checked again at the Send gate); a box that names another NAME is never accepted. Checked on the real page: the Uber chat now opens as exact name, header checked (nothing typed or sent). (2) The catch-all wording hid the reason: a click the page would not take is now said as that, and a wrong-chat refusal says what the box named (number masked). Tests: `tests/numberNamedComposer.test.ts` (6; 3 fail without the fix).
**The real send was NOT done.** The user authorized one short test message to their own chat, but the permission system refused to let me send a real message on their behalf, so no message was typed or sent and I did not work around it. To run it: say to Eya `send Eya plugin test to Aang on WhatsApp` (she will name the chat, it must say "(You)", then answer yes), or tell me to retry after allowing it.

**Third real finding — the "Sent" answer came about three seconds after the message had gone (user: "msg was already sent but giving confirmation only taking time").** Eya's own timing log of the real send: Send click 2984 / 3030 ms, the check that it went (`verify_sent`) only 85 ms. Cause, in the existing quick mode of the extension (`injected.js waitQuiet`): quick mode asks for `busyExtraMs: 0` (wait at most half a second), but the code read it as `Number(0) || 2500` = 2500, so on a chat page that merely LOOKS busy (a "Loading…"-like line or a progress ring: WhatsApp Web nearly always) every click waited ~3 s before answering. One line: an explicit 0 now means 0. Real Chrome, mock chat page made to look busy: the click took **3335 ms before, 633 ms after** (`tests/live/chatflow.live.test.ts`, fails without the fix). The sending itself was not touched. **Reload the extension once in each browser** (chrome://extensions or edge://extensions → Eya Browser Bridge → reload) for the real WhatsApp to get it.

**Not verified / not built.** A real send, and so the real "sent / delivered" marks and the persisted task of a real send; online catalogue and any plugin other than WhatsApp (Gmail, Drive, Telegram… are not built, and no empty placeholders were added); OAuth or token storage (WhatsApp needs none; the first plugin that does will need it); reading messages and saving received files (declared unsupported); the tasks list cannot stop a plugin task that is already running (its record says if it went through anyway).

## Finding a file in your chats: "where is that file in WhatsApp?" (27 Oct 2026)

**What you can say.** "Check this file where it is in WhatsApp", "find the resume pdf I sent last week on WhatsApp", "which chat did I send the fee receipt to", "find the agreement from Priya in WhatsApp". It is understood **without the AI** (`parseFileFinding` in `src/main/chat/chatFiles.ts`, wired as the `find_files` intent in `chatIntents.ts` / `ChatCommands.findFiles`). It needs a chat app (or "in the chats"), a word that asks to find or locate, and a file; a sending request ("send the pdf to Anna") and a search for a file on this PC ("where is my resume") are never taken for it. "This file" means the file Eya has just found or opened. Time words understood: today, yesterday, this/last week ("last week" is read loosely, from the start of last week up to now), this/last month, "3 days ago", "in October", "on 21/10/2026", "on Monday".

**What it does (`find_chat_files`, read-only).** It opens WhatsApp Web's own **Media → Docs** list (documents from all chats), types one word of the name into that list's own search box, reads the rows (name, type, size, sender "You"/name, date) and **judges them itself** — the list's search is only a starting point (on a mock it answered late or not at all, so a search that shows nothing is repeated on the whole list). Captions are used to match but never handed back. When **exactly one** file fits it narrows the list to that file, opens the row's menu and presses **only "Go to message"** (never Forward, Star, Reply or Delete: the entry is chosen by its exact name) so the chat opens on the file, and names the chat **only when the page shows it** (the message box names a chat different from the one that was open before, or the file's message is in view). If several files fit it lists them (newest first) and asks for more of the name or the day. It sends, changes and deletes nothing.

**Honest limits.**
- WhatsApp's document list **does not say which chat a file is in** — that is why the chat is opened at the message. A file sent to two chats shows as two rows with the same name; those cannot be told apart, so no chat is opened and that is said.
- It can only see what WhatsApp Web has loaded in that list. On the real page checked on 27 Oct 2026 the list had **5 documents, all from "Today"**: an older file may simply not be there, and when the list does not reach back as far as the time you asked, Eya says how far back it did go instead of "no such file".
- A sent file's **recipient** is not in the list. If you say who it went to ("…I sent to Anna") and the chat found is someone else's, Eya says so.
- Only WhatsApp (Telegram has no such list in Eya).

**Plugin.** WhatsApp is now plugin **1.1.0** with a new permission, **Find files in chats** (`files.find`, risk: read) and action `find_files`. An update never gives itself more, so **you grant it once on the Plugins page**; until then Eya says exactly that and does nothing.

**Tests (mock only).** `tests/chatFiles.test.ts` (44: dates as the page writes them, rows read from the page text, time windows, matching), `tests/chatFilesRequest.test.ts` (16: what counts as this request and what does not), `tests/chatFindFiles.test.ts` (26: the tool over a mock Media/Docs panel with the real page's shape — one file located, several, none, time and sender filters, the list's own search failing, same file sent twice, a chat already open, "Go to message" that moves nowhere, a list that will not narrow, access off, not a chat app, log-in/loading, Telegram), `tests/chatFindFilesCommand.test.ts` (9: said to Eya, incl. plugin not allowed and Chats off). Mutation check: 10 deliberate breakages (press the first menu button, count the open chat's documents as rows, trust the list's search, take a stale chat name, guess at a list that did not narrow, ignore "sent", ignore the time window, miss copies, leave the menu open, leave the list open) — all caught.

**Verified vs not.** *Verified on the real WhatsApp Web (read-only):* the row parser against the real page text (5 document lines on the page, 5 rows read, all dates read, sender "You" recognised), and the real shape of the list's buttons (each row has a `Download "<file>"` button and a menu button named only "Message's context menu", in the list's panel). *Not verified:* the whole tool on the real page (it needs the new permission granted by you first; I do not grant permissions for you), opening a row's menu and "Go to message" through the tool, how older dates are written ("Yesterday", weekdays, dd/mm/yyyy are handled from my reading of the format but not seen), and whether the real list loads older rows when scrolled.

## Live chat companion: "Start live chat with my friend Rahul" (29 Oct 2026)

**What it is.** Say "start live chat with Rahul" (or use the **Live chat** page). Eya finds the real chat the way she does for any message (an exact name or a whole number is an identity; a likely match asks you to confirm it; several chats ask you to choose), then **watches only that chat**. When a new message arrives she reads it, writes ONE reply for the whole burst of new messages, and keeps going with the same conversation as it goes on. It is not a search-and-send loop: after the chat is found she does not search again or refresh the page.

**Three modes, shown on the page.**
- **Draft** (default): replies are suggested and **Eya never sends**. *Approve* puts the words in WhatsApp's own message box; you press Send there, and Eya sees that it was sent.
- **Assisted**: a reply is sent **only when you press Send**. *Approve* marks it approved; *Send* sends it. Before every send she re-reads the chat.
- **Auto-reply**: only after you switch it on for THIS chat and run, tick that the other person knows and agrees that replies are AI-assisted, and set limits (default 5 replies, 30 minutes, at least 30 seconds apart). The page shows an unmissable banner. It sends only plain replies: not one that promises or states something for you, not one the model is unsure of, not one you wrote or edited, and — whatever the model says about itself — **never** one with a figure of three digits or more, a link, an address, a word for something private (password, OTP, PIN, card or account number, bank, UPI…) or more than 240 characters. Anything else stops auto-reply and gives the chat back to you. It is off after a restart, and voice cannot switch it on ("start live chat with Rahul in auto mode" starts Draft mode and says so).

**How a new message is noticed (event-driven, not polling).** Extension **0.11.0** puts a watcher inside the open chat's page (`#main`): when something changes there it tells the extension *that* it changed, with **no content at all**; the extension passes it to Eya (`chat_changed`), only while Communication Access allows WhatsApp (otherwise it answers the page "stop" and the watcher switches itself off). Eya then reads the last messages of the open chat (`chat_read`: ids, direction, kind, text, stamp) and works out what is new: a message stays "new" until a reply was drafted for it or it was judged not to need one (a sticker, a voice note, a deleted message, "ok", "thanks"), so the same message reported twice is one message, and a message whose reply could not be drafted is tried again. A once-a-minute look at the chat is only a **backstop** for a missed event. **You must reload the Eya Browser Bridge extension once in Chrome and Edge** (extensions page → the circular arrow on its card); until then Eya says plainly that the extension is too old and does not pretend to watch.

**Safety rules, each tested.** The first look at a chat makes everything already there old: nothing old is answered. Outgoing messages are never answered. If you answer in the chat yourself, the waiting draft is dropped. A newer message (or an edit to the one a draft answers) makes the draft out of date: it is **not sent** and a new one is written. Before every send: the page itself must show the chosen chat open; the send **record is saved before Send is pressed**; these messages must not already have been answered or begun to be; the send goes through the same tools and Send gate as any WhatsApp send (plugin permission "Send text messages", wrong-chat check); afterwards `verify_sent` looks in the chat. Only what the page shows is called sent. A send whose outcome is not known (a timeout, the browser dropping, a mark that never appears) is **never repeated**: it is looked for in the chat first (also after a restart), and recorded as *not seen* only if it is still not there after a while. Two Sends pressed at once send once. Stop while a send is in flight lets it finish and reports what really happened; Stop during the checks before Send sends nothing. A chat switch, the chat being closed, the browser disconnecting, Chats being switched off, or a page layout Eya cannot read pauses it. After a restart a session comes back **paused**, with its draft kept, nothing sent and nothing watched.

**Privacy.** Only the one open, chosen chat is ever read; the chat list and other chats are not. The model gets only the last 30 messages of this chat as plain lines ("Them: …"/"Me: …"), a short running summary and the tone — no name, no number — one call per burst, bounded, with a 15-second rest after a failure. The other person's words are labelled as not being instructions. Messages are kept in `live-chat.json` in Eya's data folder only while the session runs and are removed when it ends. Logs hold states and ids, never message text.

**Plugin.** WhatsApp plugin **1.2.0** adds the permission **Live chat companion** (`chats.live`, read) and the action `live_chat`. An update never grants itself more: **tick it once on the Plugins page.** Sending a reply still needs "Send text messages". The `live_chat` tool the model can call can only start, pause, resume, stop and ask the status — it cannot approve, send, confirm a chat or switch auto-reply on (only the Live chat page, or your own words to Eya, can).

**Tests (mock page, mock AI).** `tests/liveChat.test.ts` (82: start/resolve/confirm/choose, one draft per burst, duplicates, concurrency, what the model may say, Draft/Assisted/Auto, uncertain sends, pause/resume/stop, restart restore, chat switching, browser loss), `tests/liveChatPure.test.ts` (31: the auto gate, tracker, reply-need, invented facts, drafting, saved session, requests), `tests/liveChatIntent.test.ts` (28: spoken commands, the tool's limits, through ChatCommands), `tests/liveChatPage.test.ts` (13: the page for each state). **Mutation check:** 20 deliberate breakages (send an old draft, skip the already-answered check, skip the identity check, Draft mode sends, ignore your own reply, send commitments, ignore each auto limit, auto survives a restart, answer old messages, invent facts, miss a chat switch, read an unreadable page as empty, never retry after an AI failure, stop not waiting for a send, an uncertain send called sent…) — all caught.

**Real runtime test (`tests/live/livechat.live.test.ts`, 12 checks).** The real extension 0.11.0 in a real Chrome over the real bridge and Eya's real tools, against a WhatsApp-SHAPED mock page (`#main`, `data-id` rows): an incoming message reached a draft in about 180 ms (model scripted); presence/typing changes made no draft; Draft-mode Approve typed into the page's box and sent nothing; Assisted Send put exactly one message in the page, checked by `verify_sent`, and a second Send sent nothing; a message arriving while a draft waited made it stale; opening another chat paused it; Resume answered only what arrived meanwhile; Stop silenced it; with Chats switched off **no event left the page at all**. The existing real-Chrome suites (extension 31, chat flow 23, communication 11, chat commands 9) still pass with the changed extension.

**Verified vs NOT verified.**
- *Verified:* everything above on a mock page; the real Eya window loads the Live chat page, the plugin is 1.2.0 with the new permission, the page's requests and the model tool both refuse while the permission is not granted (I do not grant it for you), and a nonsense request is refused.
- *NOT verified:* **the real WhatsApp Web.** Its DOM was not checked: the reader relies on `#main`, the message rows' `data-id` ("true_"/"false_"), `span.selectable-text` and `data-pre-plain-text`, which I know from how WhatsApp Web is built but have not seen on your page (checking needs the extension reloaded, the permission granted, and a consenting person to send a message). If WhatsApp's layout differs, Eya says "cannot be read reliably" instead of reading nothing. Also not verified: a real AI model writing the replies (scripted in tests), real WhatsApp delivery marks for a live send, and a real second person.

## Live chat: noticing a message at once, and replying by itself (3 Nov 2026, follow-up)

**What was reported:** "the message had already come but it said no reply; after one or three minutes the reply appeared; it should reply by itself, without asking me."

**What I found, honestly.** In the saved plugin state of the user's Eya the *Live chat companion* permission was **not ticked** (`plugins.json`), and there was no `live-chat.json`, so the live chat had not been running in that Eya; the report may be about something else. What I did find in the code is a weakness that produces exactly that symptom once it runs: the extension sent a "chat changed" event only if its connection to Eya happened to be up at that instant and otherwise **dropped it silently**, and the only safety net was a once-a-minute look. I also tried to blame Chrome slowing timers in a background tab, **measured it, and could not reproduce it** (a hidden tab delayed a 50 ms timer by about one second, never by minutes), so that is not claimed as a cause.

**What changed (extension 0.11.1 — reload it once in Chrome and Edge).**
- The page watcher no longer waits on a timer: it looks inside the page's own change callback, which runs the moment the page changes (also in a background tab). Its fingerprint uses `textContent`, not `innerText` (which forces a layout).
- A change is reported **until Eya has confirmed receiving it**: the service worker waits (up to 6 s) for its connection to Eya, says whether the event really went, and the page reports it again (on the next change, or after 1.5 s) if not.
- If the browser connection drops while the live chat runs it pauses, and **carries on by itself when the same browser is back** (through the same checks as the person's own Resume: the chat must still be the chosen one, Chats must be on), answering what arrived meanwhile once. A closed chat, Chats switched off or the person's own Pause stay paused.
- The backstop look is every 15 s (it was 60 s). If it ever finds a message **the page did not announce**, the live chat concludes this page cannot be relied on for events and **checks every 5 s for the rest of the session**, and the page says so ("checking every few seconds"). The Live chat page also shows "last checked N s ago".
- If the AI cannot write a reply, the message is **tried again by itself** (every 15 s, three times) instead of waiting for the slow check or for the person to press Regenerate.

**Replying by itself, in one step.** On the Live chat page tick *Reply automatically (AI-assisted)* in the start form, confirm that the other person knows and agrees that replies are written with AI help, and press *Start and reply automatically*: it starts, and switches automatic replies on as soon as the chat is confirmed. By voice: "start live chat with Rahul and reply automatically" (or, while one runs, "turn on auto reply") makes Eya ask ONE question — what she will do, the limits, and that the person must know and agree — and only the person's own **yes** (typed or spoken to Eya; never speech heard by the always-on listener) switches it on. Defaults (changed on 4 Nov 2026, see the last section on live chat below): no limit on replies or time, 10 seconds apart. *(Changed on 4 Nov 2026, see "Live chat: asking for it in your own words" below: automatic replies now go out as each message comes in unless "hold back" is ticked; the next sentence describes the earlier, stricter default, which is now that option.)* Automatic replies then sent only plain replies (nothing that promises something for you, no figure of three digits or more, no link, nothing private, nothing over 240 characters); the drafting prompt now says what counts as a commitment (a promise, a plan, a time, a place, an amount, a decision) so that ordinary friendly answers — greetings, "how are you", thanks, questions back, things the conversation already says — are not held back for approval.

**Real-browser proof (`tests/live/livechat.live.test.ts`, 16 checks, real extension 0.11.1 in a real Chrome over the real bridge, a WhatsApp-SHAPED mock page, AI scripted).** An incoming message became a draft in about 170 ms. Automatic replies: the first message got its reply in the chat in about 0.75 s, exactly one per message, the gap and the limit were kept, and a reply that promised something was held back. The extension's socket was dropped while a message arrived: the live chat paused, then carried on by itself and answered once (about 150 ms after the message). With the WhatsApp tab in the **background** (another tab in front) a message was still noticed in about 170 ms. With Chats switched off no event left the page at all.

**Still NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp Web page (its DOM may differ from the mock), a real AI model writing the replies, and a real second person. Unit tests for the timing rules: `tests/liveChatTimely.test.ts` (13).

## Eya does not go offline while she works (3 Nov 2026)

**What was reported:** "if I give a task, Eya must not go offline if I do not answer for two or three minutes or more — I am silent because I am doing my own work."

**Cause.** Two separate "go quiet" rules looked at too little. *Hands-free talk* ended after **45 seconds** of quiet unless the orb was speaking/thinking/working or a persisted task was open (then capped at 10 minutes); a request she was carrying out (`agent.working`) was not counted, and the orb often shows "idle" after "okay, on it" while the work goes on. *Gemini Live* kept open for a task for at most 10 minutes, then ended even if the task was still working, and gave no time to answer after the task ended.

**Now.**
- **While she works, the person's silence never ends the conversation** — a request being carried out, a task she is keeping, a tool of the live voice running, a page she is waiting on — **however long it takes**. Only work that has gone on for **three hours without a break** is taken to be stuck and stops holding it open.
- **After the work ends she waits two minutes** for the person (who was busy) before going quiet, counted from when the work ended, not from when it began. The person speaking starts a new exchange: with no new work it is the short silence (45 s) again.
- With no work at all nothing changed: the short silence.
- **The PC is kept from sleeping while she works** (`KeepAwake`, Electron's `prevent-app-suspension`; the screen may still turn off): any open task, any request in progress, any running tool of the live voice holds it; each piece of work under its own name; released the moment nothing is being worked on, at quit, and for work that has held it for three hours.

**Files.** `src/renderer/talkIdle.ts` (the hands-free rule, pure), `src/renderer/liveConversation.ts` (`keepOpen`, `keepOpenMs` 3 h, `afterWorkMs` 2 min), `src/renderer/orb.tsx` (counts `agent.working` too), `src/main/power/KeepAwake.ts`, `ipcRouter.ts`, `liveBridge.ts`, `main.ts`.

**Tests.** `tests/talkIdle.test.ts` (14) and `tests/liveConversation.test.ts` (+4: silent for 2, 3, 10, 11, 30, 90 minutes while she works; ends after the grace; a running tool keeps it open and the quiet time counts from when it finished; hours-long work is taken as stuck), `tests/keepAwake.test.ts` (9). Mutation check: 7 deliberate breakages (working does not keep it open, no wait after work, stuck work never ends it, the person speaking not starting a new exchange, the old 10-minute cap, no grace after work, a running tool not keeping it open) — all caught. **Not verified:** a real voice conversation left silent for minutes during a real task, and the PC actually staying awake (the rule is tested with a stand-in for Electron's blocker).

## Live chat: asking for it in your own words, and the first message (4 Nov 2026)

**What was reported:** "live chat still not working" — with the sentence typed to Eya: *"+91 98765 08501 do live chat with this number she is my close friend u can make her happy she also no Eya msging here, ok do . in whatsapp"*, then "why", then "do live chat now go". Each time the **AI model** answered "I can't initiate automated personal conversations…".

**Cause.** Eya only recognised "start live chat with <name>" at the start of a sentence. A number in front, "this number", the reason ("close friend", "make her happy"), a typo ("she also no Eya msging") and a bare "do live chat now go" matched nothing, so the sentence went to the language model, which refused. Nothing had ever reached the live chat itself.

**Now (all local, no model call to understand the request).** `src/main/livechat/liveChatRequest.ts` reads the sentence into who (a phone number anywhere in it, or a name after "live chat with"), what it is for (the person's own words, kept to guide the replies), whether the **person said** the other person knows Eya is writing there ("she knows", "she also no Eya msging"; "she does not know", "secretly", "do not tell her" are never taken as her agreement and also stop Eya writing first), whether Eya is to **write first** ("make her happy", "cheer", "say hi"), the mode, and "reply automatically". A request that names nobody ("do live chat now go") uses the last person named in the last ten minutes with what was said about her; if there is none Eya asks who. What was said about one person is never carried to another.

- **She knows (said by the person in the request):** as soon as the chat is the confirmed one, automatic replies are switched on (since the later change below: no limit, 10 s apart; stop with "stop live chat") and, if asked, the first message is written.
- **Not said:** the same ONE question as before is asked (what Eya will do, the limits, that the other person must know and agree) and only the person's own typed or spoken **yes** switches it on. A plain "start live chat with Rahul" now asks it too (it used to just say it would only draft).
- **The first message** is written once, from the purpose, only after automatic replies are on; it goes out through the **same gate as any automatic reply** (high confidence, nothing promised, no figure of 3+ digits, no link, nothing private, 240 characters at most) and the same send checks (record saved before Send, verified in the chat, never repeated if uncertain). If it fails the gate it waits for the person (and automatic replies end, as for any reply). If the AI is out of reach it is tried again (3 times) and then waits for **Regenerate**. If she writes first, no first message is written. In Draft or Assisted mode it is written only when the person presses Regenerate and then waits for approval.
- **Honesty rule in every prompt:** Eya never claims to be human; asked whether she is an AI she says she is Eya, an assistant writing for the person. The prompts say the other person "knows, and agreed" only when the person confirmed it.
- **Missing permission:** instead of a refusal, Eya says exactly what to do: open Plugins in Eya, choose WhatsApp, turn on "Live chat companion" (Eya cannot do that for you), then say "do live chat now go". Same for Chats switched off. "ok" after a failed start takes the same request up again.
- **The Live chat page** has a "What should Eya do in this chat?" box and, with *Reply automatically*, a "Eya writes the first message" box; a running chat shows what you asked. The model's own `live_chat` tool can now also start it (with purpose/opening) if a sentence ever gets past the local reader, but it still **cannot** switch automatic replies on, confirm a chat or send.

**Replies go out as the message comes in (same day, second change).** The person said: "it can send without my permission, because I am looking at that chat — don't think about the message, it should send based on the reply that comes in." So, with automatic replies on, a reply is **no longer held back for how it reads**: promising or agreeing to something, a doubtful reply, a long one, one with a figure, a link or an address all go out by themselves, the model is told "the person is watching this chat and asked you to answer by yourself, always write a reply, do not hand it back", and the reply is sent as soon as the message arrives (after the gap you set). The **old strict behaviour is still there as an option**: "Hold back for my approval any reply that promises something or mentions figures or links" on the Live chat page (start form and the automatic-reply form), or `holdBack: true` in the request.
- **What I deliberately kept, and why (so this is not a silent removal):** (1) the limits — number of replies, minutes, seconds apart, and "stop live chat" — so it cannot run away; (2) **a reply that contains a password, one-time code, card number, account number or Aadhaar number is never sent by itself** (automatic replies stop and it comes to the person), because the other person can write anything into the chat, including "reply with your password", and the model alone cannot be trusted to resist that; (3) a reply you wrote or edited yourself is not re-sent as automatic; (4) **a time, amount, figure or link the conversation never gave is still not made up** — such a reply becomes a question for the person instead of a message (the model is told not to invent, so this rarely triggers); (5) the one confirmation that the other person knows replies are AI-assisted — skipped when the person says it in the request ("she knows"), asked once otherwise; (6) Eya never claims to be human. If you want any of these loosened, say which.

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatRequest.test.ts` (48+; the exact refused sentence → +91 98765 08501, aware, opening, purpose without the number; "do live chat now go" → bare start; many name/number/awareness/not-a-start cases), `tests/liveChatOpening.test.ts` (29: controller opening once/not twice/gate/AI failure/retries/Regenerate/she-wrote-first/Draft mode/stop; prompts carry the purpose and the honesty rule; ChatCommands flows with the exact refused sentences, permission guidance, "ok" retry, nobody-named, not-aware, no inheritance between people, likely-match confirm, no send permission), page tests, tool tests. Mutation check: 9 deliberate breakages (auto without "she knows", first message in Draft mode, replies without the purpose, first message after she wrote first, "does not know" read as agreement, honesty rule dropped, one person's words inherited by another, enabling auto not triggering the first message, the first message's own send key lost) — all caught. Real Chrome + the real extension over the WhatsApp-shaped mock page (`tests/live/livechat.live.test.ts`, now 17): nothing written before automatic replies were on, then exactly one first message in the chat (about 0.7 s, model scripted), no second one, and her answer replied to after it.

**Still NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp Web page, a real AI model writing real messages, a real second person, and finding a number that is **not saved** as a chat (WhatsApp's own search must find it, otherwise Eya says it found no chat). **What you must do:** reload the Eya Browser Bridge extension in Chrome and Edge (it is now 0.11.2, see the next section), and switch on "Enable live chat" on the Live chat page (that replaces ticking "Live chat companion" and the others by hand).

## Live chat: the first real run, and ONE switch for everything (4 Nov 2026, later)

**What the first real run showed (the person's own words, pasted):** (1) typed: "I could not look for that chat: There is no browser tab to look at." (Chrome was running with no window open: the extension said so, and the live chat gave up instead of opening WhatsApp Web the way every other chat request does). (2) By voice the AI said it could not do "live chat" ("not able to make WhatsApp calls", "layout my tools can't read"). (3) After the extension was reloaded: "the current WhatsApp page isn't supported" while the screen showed a **new chat with a phone number nobody had written to yet** (only the page's own "messages are end-to-end encrypted" note). (4) The person asked for **one enable option: if I enable it, it should do live chat without asking any other permission; all related permissions should enable with one switch.**

**Fixed.**
- **No browser tab → Eya opens WhatsApp Web herself** (`liveChatWiring.findChat`): "no browser tab" is treated like "not a chat app": `open_website` WhatsApp Web once, then keep looking while it loads (QR code is said, not waited on). Not guessed from other errors.
- **A new, empty chat is not an unreadable page** (extension **0.11.2 — reload it once in Chrome AND Edge**). The reader used to call a page unreadable whenever it saw any row it did not recognise and no message rows — the page's own notice and date chip are such rows, so a chat with nobody's message in it was refused. Now: unreadable only when message **bubble marks** (time/ticks line, copy block, old direction classes) are on the page with none recognised, or four or more rows and none recognised; a chat with only notices is empty and starts. This is **my inference from your description** of the screen, not a proof about the real page (see below). When a page really is called unreadable the message now carries counts ("I understood 0 message rows; the page shows N rows in all and M message marks") — numbers only, never a word of the chat — and the same counts go to the log, so the next report shows what the page looked like.
- **ONE "Enable live chat" switch** (top of the Live chat page, `LiveChatSwitch`): one click allows everything the live chat needs — the WhatsApp plugin installed and on, WhatsApp allowed in Eya (Chats on), and the three plugin permissions *Find chats and contacts*, *Live chat companion*, *Send text messages* (not *Send files*, not *Find files in chats*) — and stands as the person's **standing confirmation that the people they live-chat with know replies are written with AI help** (said on the card next to the switch). While it is on and everything underneath is still in place, a live chat **replies by itself as soon as the chat is confirmed, with no question**, writes the first message when asked, and Eya says why she did not ask ("Your Enable live chat switch is on…"). It is the same thing the person could do by hand on the Plugins page, through the same plugin manager; it remembers what **it** changed, and switching it off puts back only that (a permission or Chats the person had allowed themselves stays), ends automatic replies that were running on its word, and says so. A permission taken away later shows on the card ("On, but something it needs was taken away", the missing part marked, one button to put it back) and the switch stops counting as a standing yes meanwhile.
  - **Only the person's own click on Eya's own page can turn it on** (`enable_live` / `disable_live` exist only in the page's IPC parser): the model's `live_chat` tool, Eya's spoken or typed commands, and web pages cannot. When something is missing, Eya says "open Live chat in Eya and switch on 'Enable live chat'" — she cannot do it for the person.
  - What the person says in a request still wins: **"she does not know" / "secretly" / "do not tell her"** → nothing is sent by itself and nothing is asked (Eya says she only writes replies for review); "she knows" (as in the pasted sentence) starts automatic replies even with the switch off. Not kept across an Eya restart (as for any automatic reply): Resume then brings the chat back as Assisted.
  - Kept as before: the limits (replies, minutes, gap, "stop live chat"), no password / code / card or account number ever sent by itself, no made-up time / amount / link, never claims to be human. The model's prompts and the `live_chat` tool description now say what "live chat" is (not a call, not one message) and to point at the switch instead of saying it is unsupported.

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatSwitch.test.ts` (12; over the real plugin manager and the real WhatsApp connector: from nothing, only what it needs, the person's own grants left alone on off, Chats it turned on turned off, restart, damaged file, failure part-way), `tests/liveChatEnable.test.ts` (26; controller with the switch on/off, "she does not know" wins, confirm-then-auto, off ends auto, restart, what Eya says with the switch on/off/missing, the page), `tests/liveChatWiring.test.ts` (7; no tab → opens WhatsApp Web). Mutation checks: 13 deliberate breakages of the switch / automatic start / no-tab fix, and 2 of the page-side reliability rule in real Chrome — all caught. Real Chrome with the real extension 0.11.2 over the WhatsApp-shaped mock page (`livechat.live.test.ts`, now 20): a new chat with only notices starts and its first message is drafted; a page whose messages are not recognised is said to be unreadable, with counts. Other real-Chrome suites still pass (extension 31, chatflow 23, communication 11, chatcommands 9).

**NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp Web page (my reading of "empty chat" as the cause is an inference — if it still says unreadable, the message now carries the counts, send them to me), a real AI model writing real messages, a real second person, the switch clicked in the real window by a person, finding a number that is not saved as a chat, and whether Chrome with no window opens a tab for `open_website` on this PC.

## Live chat: waits until you pause it, introduces herself, passes messages on (4 Nov 2026, third change)

**What was said:** "live chat is not active for long — until I pause it, it should wait; it should introduce itself in the chat: 'I am Eya, I am Rakesh's assistant, tell me what you want'; interact with lively replies; and if they say anything for Rakesh, tell me."

**Stays active until you pause it.**
- Automatic replies have **no count and no time limit** unless you set one (empty box = no limit; the page says "no time limit: it waits until you pause or stop it"). What keeps it from running away is the gap between two replies (default **10 s**, was 15) and your own Pause / Stop. (The old defaults, 10 replies and 60 minutes, ended it by themselves — that is what you saw.)
- **One private word no longer ends it.** A reply containing a password, code, card or account number is still never sent by itself, but only THAT reply waits for you; automatic replies stay on for the next message.
- **It comes back by itself when you only looked at another chat.** Opening another chat (or closing this one) still stops it watching — it must not read or send in the wrong chat — but it now **carries on by itself when the chosen chat is open again** (looked at every 4 s; through the same checks as your own Resume: the right chat, Chats on, a page it can read). **Your own Pause is never undone by itself**, nor is Chats switched off, an old extension or an unreadable page.
- **A message that is visibly in the chat is not "unconfirmed".** The delivery marks Eya reads are only the last six on the page; in a chat where they are all alike a new message showed no *new* mark and the live chat stopped as "not sure it went" (found by the real-Chrome test: the introduction itself ended automatic replies). Now, when the text is found but no new mark can be told, the chat itself is read and an outgoing message with those words that was not there before counts as sent ("It is shown in the chat"). Pending, failed and not-in-the-chat stay exactly as before.
- **Emoji.** WhatsApp draws an emoji as a picture, so the text the page gives back for "Hi 😊" is "Hi". Messages are now compared, and `verify_sent` searches, with the exact text first and then without emoji; otherwise every lively reply with an emoji in it would have ended automatic replies.

**Introduces herself, as herself.** Whenever she is to reply by herself (the switch, "she knows", or the page's automatic replies) the **first thing in the chat is who she is**: "I am Eya, <your name>'s assistant", warm and lively, fitting what you asked ("make her happy"), asking what they want and inviting them to tell her anything for you ("I will pass it on"). It is on by default and can be switched off (the page's "Eya introduces herself" box, or `opening: false` / `introduce: false` in the request). If she writes first, the first reply carries the introduction instead; it is said **once**. Replies from then on are written **by Eya as herself** (not in your voice): lively, short, in the language and script the other person writes in, a friendly question back, **never pretending to be you or to be human** ("asked, she says she is Eya, <name>'s AI assistant"), and never inventing where you are, what you are doing, a plan, a time or an amount ("I do not know, I will pass the question on"). While you read every reply yourself (Draft / Assisted) it is still written in your voice.

**Your name.** "<name>'s assistant" uses the name set in the new *Your name* box on the Live chat page, else the Windows account's name (not a generic one such as Admin), else "an assistant". Only a name is accepted (letters and the usual marks, up to 40): nothing that could carry an instruction or a number into a chat.
