**Messages for you.** When the other person asks something of you, or has a message, request, question or news for you, Eya answers them ("I will tell Rakesh") and takes it down as ONE sentence (`for_owner`). It is kept in a **"For you, from the chat"** card on the Live chat page until you clear it (kept after Stop; a new live chat starts clean; at most 20; survives a restart), and you are told **with a system notification (with its sound)**: "Someone in your live chat has something for you. Open Live chat in Eya." — the notification never carries what was said. *Not built: Eya speaking it aloud by herself.*

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatPersona.test.ts` (29: the introduction in those words and once, the persona and honesty lines, Draft mode still in your voice, no-name wording, notes kept/capped/cleared/survive a restart, your name, a week of replies with no limit, your own Pause not undone, coming back after another chat / closed chat / wrong chat never / Chats off / unreadable page / Stop, the page, emoji), plus changes in `liveChat.test.ts`, `liveChatPure.test.ts`, `chatTools.test.ts`. Mutation checks: 16 deliberate breakages (limits back, one word ending it, not coming back, coming back into the wrong chat or an unreadable page, own Pause undone, voice instead of Eya, introduction every time or never, notes not kept, not told, "in the chat but no new mark" stopping it, emoji not recognised, verify without the emoji fallback): **14 caught, 2 are equivalent** (the same Chats check also sits in Resume, and the waiting tick re-checks the pause reason), so removing the duplicate changes nothing. Real Chrome with the real extension (`livechat.live.test.ts`, now 21): the introduction goes into the chat, a reply carries "for you", the person opens another chat (it waits) and back (it carries on **by itself**, then answers). Other real-Chrome suites still pass (extension 31, chatflow 23, communication 11, chatcommands 9).

**NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page (its tick marks, emoji pictures and an empty new chat are as described, not seen), a real AI model — whether it really writes lively, honest replies and fills `for_owner` well — a real second person, and Eya speaking messages for you aloud.

## Live chat: the second real run (4 Nov 2026, evening)

**What was pasted:** "do live chat with number +91 98765 08501, you can introduce yourself, respond until I say stop" → *Several chats fit … Options: 1. 12 unread messages, 2. end-to-end encrypted*; then "I opened that chat, you can do now" → "WhatsApp Web's layout isn't letting me read the messages reliably".

**What was actually wrong (three separate things, found by reading it against the code and the saved files):**
1. **The number search offered non-chats.** "12 unread messages" is screen-reader text that comes first in a chat row that has unread messages (Eya took a row's first line as its name), and "end-to-end encrypted" is a notice row inside the open conversation. Both were treated as candidate chats, so a number that matched one real chat looked like two. Fixed three ways: the extension (**0.11.3**) no longer takes an unread-badge line for a row's name; the list of things that are never somebody's name now includes unread-badge text and the encryption notice; and **a chat that is already open whose message box shows exactly the number asked for is simply that chat** (verified, nothing searched or clicked) — what "I opened that chat" means.
2. **"I opened that chat, you can do now" went to the AI**, which then told the story of a layout it never looked at. Said by voice, the AI only calls `quick_command` for what that tool is described as handling, and **live chat was not in the description** — so for the voice attempts the live chat controller was never reached at all (its saved state file did not change from 12:22 until now). The tool description and the voice prompt now name live chat and its phrases, say it is not a call or one message, and say never to claim it stopped, started or cannot read the page unless the tool said so. The text and voice paths also handle "I opened that chat / ok now go / try again" **locally**, as the same live chat request again (the last person named in the last ten minutes), in Eya's own words; a try that only got as far as asking *which* chat is replaced by the new one, while a chat that is really running is not.
3. **The reader may not know the real page's message layout** (not proven: it was never reached in this run). Extension 0.11.3 now reads a message by its own ids when they are of the known shape, and otherwise **by the bubbles' own marks** (the copy block with time and sender, the time-and-ticks line), taking the side from the page's own direction mark (the id, `message-in/out`, or the sender in the copy block compared with the chat's title). **A bubble whose side cannot be told is never read and makes the page unreadable** (answering one's own message would be worse than not answering). Also `#main` has a second name it is looked for under.

**When it still fails, it explains itself.** An "unreadable" page now says how many rows it understood and by which way, how many rows and message marks the page has, how many bubbles' sides it could not tell, and **the page's shape with every letter, digit and word masked** (kinds of elements, roles, masked ids — no message, name or number). The same counts, and why a live chat stopped or paused or could not choose a chat, are written to `%APPDATA%\eya\chat-diagnostics.jsonl` (categories and counts only), so a failure can be read afterwards even if the AI retells it wrongly.

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatRealRun.test.ts` (54: the phrases, junk labels, the local retry flow with the exact pasted sentences, the replace-an-unfinished-try rule, diagnostics notes, shape sanitising, the already-open number shortcut, what the voice AI is told). The mock WhatsApp page now has an unread-badge row, an unsaved contact shown by its number, and message layouts "ids" / "plain" (other ids, sender in the copy block) / "blind" (no way to tell the side). Mutation checks: 11 deliberate breakages, all caught. Real Chrome with the real extension 0.11.3 (`livechat.live.test.ts`, now 25): an unknown-shape page is read with correct sides and answered, a chat with unread messages is found by its name, a number already open is used, a number not open is found by the page's search and the notice rows are never offered, an unreadable page says why with a masked shape and nothing real in it. Other real-Chrome suites pass (extension 31, chatflow 23, communication 11, chatcommands 9).

**Third attempt (17:07), and what the new record showed.** The AI said "the page didn't come through clearly enough". Eya's own diagnostics for that attempt: **0 message rows understood, 16 rows and 31 message marks on the page** — and no "by bubbles" or page shape in the message, which only the new extension produces. So the browser was still running the **old extension (0.11.2)**: it had never been reloaded, and the old reader only knows messages whose ids start "true_/false_", which the real page's messages evidently do not have (31 message marks, 0 such ids). Nothing in that attempt could have worked until the extension was reloaded, and Eya did not say so. Now: **the extension advertises `chat_read_bubbles` (0.11.3 on); Eya refuses to start or read a live chat on an extension that says what it can do and lacks it**, with the exact words "the Eya extension in this browser is older than this live chat needs … reload it once on the extensions page … it is then version 0.11.3" (stopping a watcher is still allowed). And **the AI is told to pass a live chat failure on in the same words** (the voice `quick_command` result and the `live_chat` tool result now say so), because it had been shortening a precise reason into "the page wasn't clear". Tests: 11 more in `liveChatRealRun.test.ts` (63 in all); 5 mutation checks, all caught; real Chrome with the real extension still 25/25.

**NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page (all of the above rests on how it was described and on what the mock can be made to look like — the real layout is still unseen; the next failure will carry its masked shape), the voice AI actually calling `quick_command` for these phrases (its description is changed; whether it obeys is the model's), a real AI writing the replies, a real second person.

## Live chat: "couldn't read the layout" again — telling whose message is whose (5 Nov 2026, extension 0.11.4)

**What the real run showed (Eya's own record, not the AI's retelling):** extension 0.11.3 was loaded and working: **16 messages were understood by their bubbles — and one picture/voice-note bubble had no way to tell whose it was.** The 0.11.3 rule was "a bubble whose side cannot be told makes the whole page unreadable", so one picture made all 16 messages unusable. The real page has no direction in its ids or classes, so the side had to come from somewhere else.

**What 0.11.4 does instead (in this order):** the id (`true_/false_`), an old `message-in/out` class, **a delivery tick** (only a message the account itself sent has one), **where the bubble is drawn** (right of the middle = the account's own, left = the other person's; the copy block, the time line and any picture are measured, and a right-to-left page is flipped), and last the sender in the copy block compared with the chat's title. **Where the layout and the sender's name disagree, the layout wins and the disagreement is counted** (a sender can be "Rahul S." under a chat titled "Rahul Sharma"). A message that is **not text** (picture, voice note, sticker…) whose side still cannot be told is read as **received**: it has no words to answer, so as received it can only ever be "nothing to reply to", whereas calling it the person's own could make a waiting reply look as if the person had answered it. **A TEXT message whose side cannot be told is still never guessed — the page is then unreadable, as before** (answering one's own message is worse than not answering). Messages with no text spans now fall back to the copy block's own text.

**Written down, as counts only:** every started chat now records in `%APPDATA%\eya\chat-diagnostics.jsonl` how many messages' sides were told by id / class / ticks / drawn position / sender / default, and how many times the layout and the sender disagreed (`n_geo`, `n_ticks`, `n_dflt`, `n_conflicts` …) — so the next failure can be read from the file. The unreadable message also says how many sides could not be told.

**An old extension is refused with a plain message.** The extension now advertises `chat_read_sides` (0.11.4 on). Eya refuses to start or read a live chat on an extension that states what it can do and lacks it, with the exact reload steps (stopping a watcher is still allowed). **The extension must be reloaded once in Chrome AND Edge** (extensions page → reload on "Eya Browser Bridge"; it is then 0.11.4).

**Tests.** Mock WhatsApp page gets two new layouts, `modern` (plain 32-hex ids, no direction in any id or class, nested wrappers, copy block with a sender whose name differs from the header, delivery ticks, a picture with no copy block) and `modern-noticks` (the same without ticks and without text spans, so only where each message is drawn tells its side), plus a picture drawn **in the middle** (nothing tells its side). Real Chrome with the real extension 0.11.4: `livechat.live.test.ts` **27/27** — both layouts are read with every text message on its correct side, the middle picture is read as received and counted, the next message is drafted. 6 mutation checks on the reader (position not used, non-text default removed, words not read without text spans, ticks not used, sender believed over layout, sides swapped) — all caught; 3 more on the unit side (any name let through as a count, a negative count let through, side counts not written when a chat starts) — all caught. `liveChatRealRun.test.ts` now 65. Full unit run **3,879 passed** (222 skipped = the opt-in real-browser suites); other real-Chrome suites pass singly (extension 31, chatflow 23, communication 11, chatcommands 9, extension errors 3). `tsc` clean, `electron-vite build` clean.

**NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page — the sides rule rests on how the page was described by Eya's masked diagnostics (16 messages, ids of the plain shape, no direction marks) and on what the mock can be made to look like; whether the real page draws its bubbles left/right as assumed, whether it shows ticks on the account's own messages in that structure, and whether a real picture bubble is read as received are all unproven. If it still fails, the diagnostics file now says which of these did not hold. Also not verified: a real AI writing the replies, a real second person, the voice AI calling `quick_command` for these phrases.

**Update (6 Nov 2026): the user ran it on the real WhatsApp Web with extension 0.11.4 and it worked** — their screenshots show the live chat confirmed on the number, "Watching for new messages", 6 replies sent in that session, automatic sending on, the person's messages for them taken down under "For you, from the chat", and the tasks "Send a message on WhatsApp — Sent, and the chat showed it". That is the user's own run (seen in their screenshots); it was not run by the assistant, and the page's layout is still known only through those results and Eya's masked diagnostics.

## The window, tidied: everything reachable, nothing on top of anything (6 Nov 2026)

**What the user pointed at (five screenshots):** the Plugins page and the Live chat page were cut off at the bottom with no way to scroll to the rest; with many tasks the Tasks page drew the tasks on top of the tabs; the chat box had a second "Eya" logo and name although the sidebar already shows Eya; the sidebar's chat history was squeezed to one or two rows by the Connected Apps block under it.

**What was wrong (found by reading the styles, then by running the app):** the content area of the window clips whatever does not fit (`overflow: hidden`) and only some pages scroll by themselves — Plugins and Live chat did not. On the Tasks page both cards were told to shrink to nothing (`flex: 1; min-height: 0`), so the task list spilled over the tabs under it. The sidebar's free space was shared half-and-half between the chat list and an invisible spacer.

**What changed (all in the page's look, nothing in what Eya does):**
- **Plugins and Live chat scroll** (wheel, scroll bar) and end with room under the last card.
- **Tasks:** the tasks card has its own scroll (at most about a third of the window), the tabs stay under it, the history list under the tabs takes the rest and scrolls inside its card; on a short window the whole page scrolls instead of squeezing.
- **Home:** the duplicate Eya logo, name and status line in the chat box are gone (the sidebar still shows Eya).
- **Sidebar:** the **Connected Apps block is off by default** — it is one switch in the Layout menu ("Connected Apps (sidebar)"), and the choice is remembered. People who already had saved layout choices get it hidden **once** (a one-time marker), and a switch turned on afterwards is kept. The chat list now gets all the room that is left (a divider above it, a thin scroll bar, a little less height per menu row): **7 chats visible on a full-screen window, 3 on an 860 px window (it was 1–2)**.
- **Two small things found while looking:** the permission switches on Plugins were squeezed to a different width on every row (they keep one size and line up now), and the form fields on the Live chat page had no visible box (and one was cut short); they have a box and the full width. Looks only — the live chat's logic was not touched.

**Tests.** `tests/uiPrefs.test.ts` (the defaults, the one-time hiding, a later choice kept, a first opening that does not force it later). A real run of the built app in a throwaway copy (own data folder, no microphone, own bridge port, fake AI addresses, 21 invented chats and 14 invented tasks) with real wheel input and screenshots: 42 checks — no Eya header on Home, Connected Apps off and switchable and remembered, chat list size at two window heights and reachable to the last chat, Plugins and Live chat scroll to their last option/card at two window heights, switches equal, fields boxed, Tasks card above the tabs above the list at two heights with every task reachable, a 60-row history scrolls inside its card. Full unit run passes (203 files; the opt-in real-browser suites skipped), `tsc` clean, `electron-vite build` clean.

**NOT verified:** the user's own screen (their window size and scaling; checked at 1360×860, 1360×560–620 and 1920×1030 at 125% scaling), a very long list of real activity items on the Tasks page (60 rows of the same markup were put in by hand for the layout check — the history itself lives in a database that was not filled), dark mode (only the light theme was looked at). The running Eya of the user was **not restarted** (it was in a live chat), so it shows the old window until it is next started.

## Live chat: stops when they go quiet, takes pictures and documents, passes things on aloud, sounds like a person (6 Nov 2026, extension 0.11.5)

**What was asked:** unlimited messages until the user stops it, or until the other person has been silent for more than 5 minutes (then it stops by itself); if the other person sends a picture or a document, download it (into the Downloads folder only), read it, and ask why it was sent / whether anything needs changing, or say "I will tell Rakesh"; whatever they want passed on must be spoken to Rakesh (who it is from — the number or the name — and what they said), and whatever Rakesh says must be answered to that person; and the messages must read like a real person typed them, small, and make the other person feel happy.

**1. It stops by itself after 5 minutes of silence — and nothing else limits it.** The count starts when the live chat begins and restarts whenever something happens in the conversation: they write, a reply of Eya's goes out, the person types in the chat themselves, the person dismisses a waiting reply, or Eya judges a message needs no answer ("ok", "thanks"). It only ends it when it is really *their* turn: nothing of theirs is waiting for an answer, no reply is waiting for the person to approve it (ten minutes of approving is not their silence), nothing is being written or sent, and the first message that was asked for has gone. Just before ending it Eya takes one last look at the chat, so a message that arrived a moment ago and was not announced yet is never mistaken for silence. A paused chat is not stopped (resuming starts the count afresh). When it stops it says so ("Stopped by itself: … did not write for 5 minutes. Say "do live chat" to start it again.") and tells the person. **This reverses the earlier rule "it waits until I pause it"**: there is still no limit on how many messages or how long it runs while they keep writing (tested: 40 replies over more than two hours of conversation), but a conversation that has gone quiet is over. The 5 minutes is a constant (`IDLE_STOP_MS`), shown on the Live chat page; there is no setting for it yet.

**2. Pictures and documents.** A picture or document the other person sends is, before the reply is written: (a) *a picture* — taken from what the page holds (its own bytes), checked by its first bytes (never by its name or type), and written to the Downloads folder as "WhatsApp picture <date> at <time>.<ext>", never over an existing file; (b) *a document* — its name is read from the bubble and judged **before anything is pressed**: only pictures and ordinary documents (pdf, doc/docx, xls/xlsx, ppt/pptx, txt, rtf, csv, odt) are taken, **never a program, script, archive, shortcut or installer**; then the bubble's own download button is pressed once and the browser downloads it; what the browser saved is checked afterwards (it must be in the Downloads folder, be a type that is taken, and not be bigger than 15 MB) — otherwise it is left exactly where the browser put it, unopened, and said so. The saved file is read through the existing `analyze_document` tool (only a file in Downloads is offered to it; Excel and PowerPoint cannot be read and are said to be "saved, but I could not read it"). The reply is then written from what was found (shown to the model as the other person's content, with brackets and line breaks stripped, never as an instruction): she says she got it and what it seems to be, kindly asks why they sent it and what they want done with it or whether anything needs to change, and says she will tell Rakesh. Rakesh is told where the file is and what it is. At most three files per burst are looked at; a file is never fetched twice; a file that cannot be got at is said so ("I could not open it") and the chat goes on. Nothing is ever opened, run, changed, moved, deleted, signed or forwarded. It uses the live chat's own permission (no new permission) and only while Chats is on. **Needs the extension reloaded (it is then 0.11.5).**

**3. What they say for Rakesh is spoken to Rakesh, and Rakesh's answer is sent back.** When the other person says something for Rakesh, the note (kept on the Live chat page as before) is now also **said aloud and shown in the chat with Eya**: "Rakesh, Priya says: she asks you to call her tonight. Tell me what to answer, if you like." A phone number is spoken by its last four digits only ("the number ending 5 5 3 2") and shown masked, never read out in full; with voice off it is shown in the chat and a notification is raised; in Talk mode the live voice model is asked to tell it. To answer, Rakesh says or types **"tell her I will call at 6"** (also "tell him that…", "tell them…", "let her know…", "reply to her: …", "reply: …", "tell her to call me" → "please call me"): it is sent in that chat as **"Rakesh says: I will call at 6"** and said back. This works only while a live chat is running (otherwise "tell him …" keeps meaning a message to the person you messaged earlier — that earlier feature was briefly shadowed and the regression was caught by the existing test); "reply: …" without "her" only counts within 30 minutes of something they said for Rakesh. The person's words are the approval, so it is not held back for one, but every other send check stays (the chosen chat is the open one, Send is permitted, a record is kept before Send is pressed, the message is looked for in the chat afterwards). A newer message from them does not drop it (it is not an answer to anything); that message is answered after it. In Draft mode nothing is sent: the words go in WhatsApp's own message box for the person to press Send. Always-on listening never relays.

**4. It sounds like a person.** The model is told, for the introduction, every reply and a first message, to write like a real person typing on a phone: usually one line (never more than two short ones), casual and warm, everyday words and contractions, a natural reaction ("aww", "oh nice", "haha"), at most one emoji, matching their style and length, making them feel good — and never like customer service or an AI (no "How may I assist you", lists, headings, bold marks, long dashes, repeating their words, piles of exclamation marks). The words that say who she is are untouched: she still introduces herself once as Eya, Rakesh's assistant, and never claims to be human. And whatever the model writes is tidied before it is a draft (`tidyReply`): marks, bullets, long dashes and stacked "!!!" go, at most two emoji stay, and a long reply keeps its first whole sentences (≤ 280 characters); it never adds a word.

**Tests.** Unit: `liveChatIdle` (12), `liveChatRelay` (32), `liveChatMedia` (60), `liveChatHuman` (16); the whole unit run passes (4,001; the opt-in real-browser suites skipped), `tsc` clean. **Real Chrome with the real extension 0.11.5** (`tests/live/livechatmedia.live.test.ts`, 9): a picture the page holds as a blob is saved in a throwaway Downloads folder as a real PNG, a document's own download button is pressed once and the browser's download lands in it, a program (`setup.exe`) is **not** downloaded and its button is not pressed, a picture that has not loaded writes nothing, the account's own message and a text message are refused; all other real-Chrome suites still pass singly (live chat 27, extension 31, chat flow 23, communication 11, chat commands 9, extension errors 3, autopair 4, sessions 15, shop 7, browser control 8, cart 6, busy 2). Mutation checks: 19 deliberate breakages of the new controller, media, relay, tone and parsing logic and 4 of the extension's media operation — each makes the tests fail (one first slipped through, which added the test that a file is fetched once when the AI fails and retries; another found that Eya's own just-sent reply delayed the stop; a third found that "tell him …" had been taken from messages to somebody).

**Found by running it:** (1) Chrome allows only the **first** automatic document download in a tab: a second one without anyone touching the page did **not** start in the real-Chrome test, and Eya said "I could not open it: the download did not start" and carried on (the person's own click on the page resets Chrome's limit; pictures are not affected, they are taken from the page, not downloaded). (2) A control named "audio-download" would make the reader call a document an audio message; the mock uses "download" — what the real page uses is not known.

**NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page's picture and document bubbles (the reader and the extension look for a loaded picture held as a blob/data address and for a control named for downloading; if the real page differs, Eya says she could not open the file and the diagnostics record which icons the bubble showed — nothing else breaks); reading real files with the real AI (scripted in these tests; the reader itself is the existing tool); the **voice** actually speaking a note (the handler in the page is type-checked and the text is tested, but no speech was run); the "tell her …" phrase said by voice to the live model; how a real model sounds (the instructions are tested, not its behaviour); a real second person.

## Live chat: she keeps them engaged — "ok, I will tell him, anything else?", and a check-in before a quiet chat ends (6 Nov 2026)

**What was asked:** "when the other person takes time to answer, the live chat is gone; and when they say something to tell Rakesh, she should say ok, then what else, and keep them engaged in the chat."

**1. Taking something down for Rakesh never leaves them with nothing.** As Eya, the model is now told, for anything the other person wants passed on, to ALWAYS write a reply: say warmly that she will tell Rakesh ("ok, I will tell Rakesh") and, in the same message, keep the chat going with one small friendly question (whether there is anything else they want her to tell him, or something kind about what they said); never to wait for Rakesh, never to say the chat is over. If the model still writes no reply — it noted something for Rakesh, or handed a question back — it is asked **once more** to write the reply (a second call, only as Eya, only when something was taken down or a question was handed back); if that fails or is empty, what it first said stands (the note for Rakesh is kept once, nothing is made up, no error is shown). Rakesh is told as before (aloud and on the screen) and **may take as long as he likes: nothing waits for him** — no reply of Eya's to them needs his approval in automatic mode.

**2. A word that answers her own question is answered.** "yes", "no", "ok", "sure" right after a message of hers that was a question ("Anything else you want me to tell Rakesh?") is an answer, so it gets a reply instead of being dropped as the closing "ok" of a chat. Only the very next message counts (an old question does not turn a later "ok" into an answer); "thanks" and a thumbs-up still need none; after a plain statement "ok" still needs none.

**3. A quiet chat is checked on once before it ends.** *(Updated the same day: the silence is now **15 minutes**, not 5 — see the section after this one. The text below describes the mechanism; read "5 minutes" as "15 minutes" and "about 10 minutes in total" as "about 30".)* The 5-minute silence is still counted exactly as before. When it is up and automatic replies are on, Eya no longer just ends the chat: she sends **one** short friendly line ("Still around? Anything else you want me to tell Rakesh?") in the other person's language — written by the model in a prompt that says it is a gentle check-in, never a complaint, never "you took long" or "the chat will end" — through exactly the same checks as every automatic reply (the same gate, the same record before Send, the same look at the chat afterwards). The silence is then counted again from that line: if they stay quiet for another 5 minutes the chat ends ("Stopped by itself: … did not write back, even after I checked in, for 5 more minutes"). If they write, anything goes on and a later silence gets its own check-in; anything else Eya sends (a reply, Rakesh's own words through "tell her …") also gives them a new chance. No check-in goes out when: automatic replies are off (a check-in would only sit waiting for approval, so the chat ends after 5 minutes as before), the AI cannot write one (the chat ends after 5 minutes as before), the gate refuses it (a word for something private), or a message of theirs / one the person typed in the chat arrived while it was being written (then that message is answered and the chat goes on). **Total silence before it ends in automatic mode is therefore about 10 minutes (5, a check-in, 5 more).**

**Found by running it:** while the check-in was being written, a message arriving at that moment made the normal reply path draft its own reply and collide with the check-in's send; the check-in now holds the "a look is in progress" flag during the model call (changes are noted and looked at straight after), a check-in that a newer message made out of date counts as "they are back", not "nothing sent → end it", and the watch for silence is set again.

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatEngage.test.ts` (22), plus a real-Chrome test in `tests/live/livechat.live.test.ts` (28 in the file): the real extension puts the check-in into the WhatsApp-shaped page, a "yes" to it is answered, a new silence gets its own check-in and a silence after that ends the chat with the right words. 19 deliberate breakages of the new logic are each caught by the tests. **NOT verified:** how a real model words the acknowledgement or the check-in (the instructions are tested, not its behaviour), the real WhatsApp page, a real second person, the 5-minute length in real time (12 seconds stood in for it in the real-Chrome test).

## Live chat through the small local model, and the 15-minute check-in (6 Nov 2026, third pass)

**What was asked:** "make the check-in wait 15 minutes; check whether there are still bugs in understanding the chat and giving proper replies — it is not how the local models give replies."

**15 minutes.** `IDLE_STOP_MS` is now 15 minutes (it was 5). In automatic mode: 15 minutes of silence → ONE friendly check-in → another 15 minutes of silence → the chat ends and says so. About 30 minutes in total. Assisted and Draft modes (no check-in is possible there) now end after 15 quiet minutes instead of 5. The page text follows the number by itself. The longest gap a person can set between two automatic replies (10 minutes) is now shorter than the quiet time, so it can never hold a check-in back. *(I read "the check-in waits 15 minutes" as both the silence before it and the silence after it; they are one constant. Tell me if you meant only one of them.)*

**The bug, measured on the real model (Ollama, llama3.2:1b).** Every live-chat answer from the local model was unreadable: 8 of 8 test messages came back as "I could not read the AI's answer", and in automatic mode that put the chat into "I need you for this one" — the other person got nothing and the chat waited for the person. Causes, all found by running the real model and looking at what it was actually sent:
1. The local provider replaces every prompt with a short generic "reply in plain text" one (`compactForLocal`) — which threw away the live chat's own instructions (the JSON shape, Eya's voice, the rules). The model answered in plain text, never the JSON.
2. That generic prompt also cut the last message from the FRONT at 1,500 characters, so on a long chat the new message was the part that was lost.
3. When it did write something it made things up: "I told Rakesh that you called, and he's now online", "I am a real person" (to "are you a bot?"), "nobody is free right now", and the note for the person was sometimes its own refusal copied over, or the word "None".

**What changed.**
- **A structured-task option** (`CompleteOptions.structured`: a JSON Schema + the messages written for a small model) travels through the chain to Ollama. Ollama then keeps the task's own prompt (no generic replacement, no clipping), is held to the JSON shape (`format`), and marks its answer `local`. The cloud models ignore the option and get the full prompt exactly as before. The other person's words no longer decide which AI answers a structured task.
- **A prompt made for a 1B model** (`buildLocalDraftMessages`, nudge and opening versions): a few hundred words, four examples, only the last 8 lines of the chat (her side labelled "Eya"), the newest message always last.
- **Checks on every reply, whichever model wrote it** (`messages.ts`): nothing about where Rakesh is or what he will do ("he is online", "Rakesh will call you", "I told Rakesh"; "I do not know if he is free" is fine); never "I am a real person / not a bot / Rakesh"; not their own words said back; not her last message again; the word for "nothing" ("None", "null", "N/A") is nothing; a note for Rakesh that is really her own speech is dropped.
- **A message for Rakesh the model missed is still handed on.** The words themselves are checked too ("tell Rakesh …", "let him know", "ask her to …", "call me back", a question about him by name); "tell ME Rakesh's password" is deliberately NOT one. It only ever adds a note, in their own exact words.
- **A firmer standard for the small model only** (the completion says it is local): its reply to something for Rakesh is replaced by the plain true line ("Ok, I will tell Rakesh. Is there anything else you want me to tell Rakesh?", varied a little so two in a row differ; "I don't know that, but I will ask Rakesh…" for a question about him); its first message must say who she is (put in front if forgotten); its opening is asked again once, then the plain opening.
- **A failing model no longer leaves them with nothing (Eya mode only).** An answer that is unreadable, made up, claims something about Rakesh, claims to be human, echoes, or repeats her last message is asked again ONCE, told exactly what was wrong; if that fails too (or the second call errors), a plain honest line goes ("Sorry, I did not catch that properly. Could you say it again?" / the pass-on line / "I'm Eya, an AI assistant writing for Rakesh…"), unless it would be the very line she sent last (then the person is asked, as before). A repeat that repeats again is simply not sent. A check-in that nags ("I've been waiting for your call") or cannot be used is replaced by the plain friendly one. In the person's own voice (Assisted/Draft) nothing changes: an unusable answer is handed back to the person.

**Real model, after the change (llama3.2:1b, this PC, CPU):** every one of the 14 cases gets a readable answer and a reply that makes no claim about Rakesh and invents nothing; pass-ons carry a note with the person's words; a first call takes about 7–13 s (the model loads), later ones 2–8 s. **How good the wording is stays limited by a 1B model:** it does not understand Telugu or Hindi written in English letters (it answers "I don't know, what do you mean?" or echoes them, which is then caught and answered with "I did not catch that"), its small talk is generic, and it copies its examples. A larger local model, or Gemini, will sound better; the checks above hold for whichever model answers.

**A promise has something behind it.** If a reply says she will tell or ask Rakesh, the other person's words are noted for him (a small model copied "I will ask Rakesh" into a plain greeting, which would have been a promise with nothing behind it); the reply itself stays as written.

**Tests.** Full unit suite 4,070 passed (was 4,023), tsc clean, real-Chrome live chat 28/28 and media 9/9, and 40 deliberate breakages of the new logic are each caught. `tests/liveChatLocalModel.test.ts` (39: the checks, the pipeline, the plain lines, the check-in/opening, and two runs through the controller), 3 more in `tests/liveChatEngage.test.ts` (the real 15-minute value), 5 in `tests/ollamaProvider.test.ts` (the structured task reaches Ollama untouched, and through the chain), 3 older tests updated to the new "plain line instead of silence" behaviour, and an opt-in real-model run `EYA_LIVE_OLLAMA=1 npx vitest run tests/live/livechat.localmodel.live.test.ts` (add `EYA_LIVE_OLLAMA_SHOW=1` to read what it said). **NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page, a real second person, cloud models' behaviour with the new checks (only scripted), any model other than llama3.2:1b, languages other than English in the real model.

## Live chat: Eya lives in the chat like a friend, and a real PDF is finally taken (6 Nov 2026, evening)

**What was seen:** two reports from a real live chat — (1) "she replied the first time, then nothing reached WhatsApp (Eya's own page still showed replies)", and (2) an exported chat with a friend of Rakesh's: "Eya behaves like a full AI, every time saying what she should tell Rakesh, not living in the chat; she should make the person happy, and download, open and analyse the files they send."

**1. One failed Send no longer ends automatic replies.** The terminal log showed the second reply's Send fail 0.5 s after it was pressed, and that ONE failure switched automatic mode off — after that every reply was only a draft waiting for approval in Eya, and WhatsApp showed nothing (until pause/resume). Now the same reply is tried again 4 s later (after looking at the chat, so a message that did go is never sent twice); a reply for which the chat could not be read just before sending is tried again too; automatic mode ends only after 3 failures in a row, and says what failed and how many times. The reason is now written to the log (`a send did not go`) — it was not before, so the cause of that first failure is **still unknown**.

**2. She lives in the chat.** The real chat showed almost every reply ending "Anything else you'd like me to pass on?" — that came from a rule I had written that morning ("keep the chat going with a question, for example whether there is anything else to tell Rakesh"), which the model copied. Rewritten: she is a warm, cheerful friend in the conversation — takes part in whatever they talk about (health, family, food, festivals), comforts, celebrates, asks ONE natural question about THEIR topic (not every time), mentions Rakesh only when it matters, says "I'll let Rakesh know" once and lightly, and never asks "anything else to tell Rakesh?". The first message joins a conversation that is already going on instead of opening with an offer to take messages (which confused the friend: "Why u asked like this?"). If asked about Rakesh she says she cannot speak for him; if asked about herself, she answers honestly (an AI assistant; she does not know how she was built; she cannot see Rakesh's computer).

**3. She knows whose lines are whose.** In a live chat both Eya's messages and the ones Rakesh types himself are "sent by the account". The lines are now labelled `Eya:` and `Rakesh:` (the controller knows which ones it sent), and the prompt says what the labels mean, so a friend's "is this text by Rakesh or his assistant?" can be answered. (The small local model is not shown Rakesh's lines: labelled, it answered the friend as if the friend were Rakesh.)

**4. Documents: a real bug, found by the real chat.** A document's bubble always has words in it (its file name, its size), and the extension's reader decided "has words → a text message" before anything else, so a PDF was never recognised as a document and nothing was downloaded; Eya saw a message called "07 - … .pdf" and said she did not have the tools. **Extension 0.11.6** recognises a document by what it offers (a document icon or download control, plus a file name) BEFORE looking at its words; if the page draws the download arrow with no name, the document's own button is pressed; and Eya will not start a live chat on an older extension (capability `chat_documents`) — **reload the extension in Chrome AND Edge**. The mock WhatsApp page in the tests now has the real shape (words in the bubble, an unnamed arrow); with the fix reverted, the real-Chrome test fails exactly as the real chat did. If a download still does not start on the real page, Eya now says which icon names the page showed (names only), and logs them.
 - She talks about a file like a friend: what it is, what stood out, one question (why they sent it / whether they want it explained). She never says she cannot open files; when a file really could not be read she says that simply and asks what it is.
 - Reading: PDFs and pictures still go to Gemini. When that has no allowance left, a plain text, CSV, Markdown, JSON, log or Word file is read right here (its first words), so she is not telling someone she cannot read a file she opened. No PDF reader was added.
 - Chrome allows only ONE automatic download per tab; a second document sent in a row in the same WhatsApp tab may not start (Eya then says "the download did not start"). Allowing "Automatic downloads" for web.whatsapp.com in Chrome's site settings removes that.

**5. The small local model, replayed on the real chat** (`tests/live/livechat.replay.live.test.ts`, with the export read from a path — never kept in the repo): it invented a person ("a hard worker… I've known him for a long time"), repeated itself three times, wrote junk notes for Rakesh, and copied the "pass it on" example for a PDF. For the small model only: any statement about the person is a claim ("nobody is free right now" too), near-repeats of her last three messages are asked again (and she asks for "a bit more" rather than going quiet), notes for Rakesh only when the words themselves ask for something to be handed on, a reply to a file is built from what the reader found, and a good reply that already says "I'll let Rakesh know" is kept. Its answers are still those of a 1B model: fine for a short friendly line, weak in a long or multi-person conversation, and poor in Telugu/Hindi/Tamil written in English letters. Gemini (or a larger local model) will sound better.

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatFriend.test.ts` (24), `tests/liveChatSendRetry.test.ts` (10), the real-Chrome media suite (10; fails with the fix reverted), the persona/engage/local-model tests moved to the new wording, 15 deliberate breakages of the new drafting logic caught (see the report), 12 of 13 for the send retry (the 13th is equivalent). **NOT verified:** the new persona text against a REAL cloud model (Gemini's free daily allowance was used up the same evening — rerun the replay after it resets), the real WhatsApp document bubble (the mock now has the real shape, the real page may still differ), a real PDF read, the cause of the one failed Send.

## Already signed in: Eya now sees it, carries on, and adds to the cart (7 Nov 2026)

**What was seen:** a pasted run — "open swiggi and login with phone number …" got "I can't access Swiggi … I suggest you log in to your account" (the AI model's own refusal; neither the browser nor the search was the problem); "login filpkart and order POCO M6 Pro 5G 6GB/128GB any colour" opened the product page and said "the page is ready for you to sign in and place the order". Rakesh was already signed in to both in his own Chrome; Eya did not recognise it, did not carry on, and never added to the cart.

**Causes found.** (1) The only sign-in signal Eya had was a visible password box. Flipkart and Swiggy sign in with a phone number and a one-time code, so a signed-in page and a signed-out page looked the same to her. (2) The typo'd names ("swiggi", "filpkart") skipped the step where the application itself opens the named site before the model is asked, so the model improvised — and guessed or refused. (3) Nothing told the model that adding to a cart spends nothing, so it stopped at the product page.

**What changed.**
 - **Signed in or not, in every page result** (`src/main/browser/signInState.ts`): `signedIn: yes | likely | no` plus `signInEvidence` (what was seen), worked out from the page's links, buttons, clickable elements, the links inside a closed account menu, dialogs and headings. A Logout anywhere (also inside the closed menu) = yes; a Login / Sign in control, a password box, or a phone/email box with a Login-style button = no; "Hello, <name>" = likely; a full page of a known shop/food/travel site with no Login control = likely; anything else says nothing (Eya then looks and never guesses). "My Profile / Orders" are NOT evidence — Flipkart lists them under Login even when signed out.
 - **Account sites, typos allowed** (`src/main/agent/accountSites.ts`): 16 known sites (Flipkart, Amazon, Swiggy, Zomato, Myntra, Meesho, BigBasket, Blinkit, Zepto, BookMyShow, IRCTC, MakeMyTrip, Goibibo, RedBus, Nykaa, Ajio, Paytm), with the usual misspellings. The request needs an action word and is told apart as login / order / plain open; short names match only exactly or by a listed misspelling, so "order tomato soup" is not Zomato and "buy a mantra book" is not Myntra.
 - **The application opens the site first** (`discovery.ts`) and tells the model the facts: already signed in → "do NOT ask them to sign in"; signed out with a phone number given → type those ten digits into the phone box (a phone number is not a password), ask Rakesh for the one-time code, never refuse; unknown → look at the page. "Login to Swiggy" on a signed-in page, and a plain "open swiggy", are answered by Eya herself with what she saw and no model call ("I opened Swiggy, and you're signed in.").
 - **A refused login is corrected** (`browserClaims.ts`): when a login was asked and the model answers "I can't log in / I need your credentials", the answer is not shown; the model is told a login request is an ordinary request (max 2 corrections).
 - **Orders go on to the cart** (`prompts.ts`): search, pick the variant (any colour when "any colour"), press Add to cart (that spends nothing), open the cart, read the totals — and BEFORE the click that places the order or pays, tell Rakesh the item, price, total and where it goes, and wait for his yes. The spend gate in `sensitiveActions.ts` is unchanged; passwords, card and bank details remain his to type.
 - **Unchanged on purpose:** "open movierulz" is still refused — that is Eya's own rule about piracy sites, not a bug.

**Tests.** `tests/signInState.test.ts` (18, shapes taken from the real public pages), `tests/accountSites.test.ts` (19, including Rakesh's literal requests, false-positive guards and engine-level runs with no model call), real-Chrome `tests/live/signin.live.test.ts` (4: nobody in → no, name + account menu with Logout → yes, clickable "Sign In" div → no, same answer from the browser snapshot), plus the whole suite (4,144 passed). 20 deliberate breakages of the new code were all caught. Run live: `EYA_LIVE_BROWSER=1 npx vitest run tests/live/signin.live.test.ts`.

**NOT verified:** the real signed-in Flipkart / Swiggy pages in Rakesh's own Chrome (only the signed-out public pages were read; the signed-in state was never seen); Swiggy draws its header late, so an early look may say nothing and Eya re-reads; "likely" for a page with no Login button is an inference; a real model carrying a real order to the cart end to end. If a page is still misjudged, the tool result now carries `signedIn` and `signInEvidence`, so the evidence can be read in the chat diagnostics.

## Ordering on Flipkart: what the real run showed, and the three fixes (7 Nov 2026, evening)

**What was seen (read from the terminal log of a real run, not guessed):** after the sign-in work, Rakesh tried again: "it is still not ordering, still not finding whether the page is signed in, and it is slow". The log showed three separate things, none of them the sign-in check:

1. **A slow AI model ended the whole request.** The first Gemini model (gemini-3.8-flash) hung for its full 10 s; that used up the round's 8 s budget, so the other Gemini models (which answer in 1.5–3 s) were never asked, Gemini was put to rest, and the request went on to OpenAI (no credit), Groq (request too large for its 8,000-token limit), OpenRouter (key refused) and then the small local model (llama3.2:1b). The local model took 30–35 s per step and, in the first request, answered "I cannot access the user's account…", which ended the task. Another request used 146 s and 299,000 tokens.
2. **"Buy at ₹14,249" was "not visible on the page".** On the real Flipkart product page (read with the built-in browser) the buy buttons are plain `<div>`s: no role, no tabindex, `cursor: auto`. Eya's list of what can be clicked only held marked-up controls and `cursor: pointer` ones, so a page whose only way to buy was such a box looked like it had none. There is also no "Add to cart" at all on that phone page: the way to buy is the "Buy at ₹…" box (it starts checkout).
3. **The exact phone was not there.** Flipkart's search for "POCO M6 Pro 5G 6GB 128GB" returned only screen guards and cases for it, plus newer models (M7 Pro, M8x …). A model searching "M7 Pro" instead is a silent swap of what was asked for.

**What changed.**
 - **AI chain** (`GeminiAIProvider.ts`, `OllamaAIProvider.ts`, `ChainedAIProvider.ts`): at least TWO Gemini models are always tried before Gemini is given up; in a hand-over call a model that has not answered in 4 s (`failFastHedgeMs`) is raced by the next one, so a hung model costs 4 s, not 10 + 30; the small local model no longer carries a task on (a request with tool results in it, unless the person chose "local only"): the cloud AI's own error is reported, or, if nothing else was asked, the plain sentence "My cloud AI did not answer in time … say 'continue'".
 - **Extension 0.11.7** (`injected.js`, pass "2b"): a short call-to-action text (Buy at ₹…, Buy now, Add to cart, Place order, Proceed, Continue, Pay, Login …) in an element with no children that sits on a painted, button-sized box is listed and clicked like a button. A sentence that merely starts with "buy" and a green badge are not. **Reload the extension in Chrome AND Edge** (chrome://extensions → reload on Eya). "Buy now" and "Place order" still go through the spend gate (asks first).
 - **Eya looks at the screen herself** (`agent/autoLook.ts`, Settings → "Look at the screen by herself", ON by default): when a click or fill finds nothing, at once for a buying / cart / checkout / sign-in word, otherwise on the second miss in a row, at most twice per request, Eya looks at the person's own Chrome or Edge window (a picture read by Gemini, never saved; `describe_screen` still refuses a chat app that Communication Access is off for) and puts what it shows into the failed step's result as `screenLook`. She says in the chat that she is looking. It only looks; it does not click, and it never asks. The continuous Screen Share was not started: a text task cannot use a stream of pictures, only a one-time look.
 - **Order notes**: phones may have no Add to cart (press Buy at ₹… after choosing the variant); if the exact model is not in the results say so, name the closest with its price, and ask before choosing it; confirm the item, price and total before the click that places the order or pays. The sign-in answer Eya read (`signedIn`, `evidence`) is now written in the log line "discovery by the application".

**Tests.** `tests/autoLook.test.ts` (10), `tests/backupAi.test.ts` (+3: hung first model, race, two-model rule), `tests/ollamaProvider.test.ts` (+3), real-Chrome `tests/live/ctabox.live.test.ts` (4; page shaped like Flipkart's), settings tests; whole unit suite green; all 17 real-Chrome suites that use the extension green; 13 + 3 deliberate breakages caught (two survived at first, and showed two weak tests that were then fixed).

**NOT verified:** the real Flipkart page in Rakesh's own signed-in Chrome — whether a click on its real "Buy at" box starts checkout (a synthetic click on the public page did nothing visible, signed out); the real Gemini picture read of a browser window; a real order run to the checkout page. Per-step time is still about 3–4 s because each step carries about 20,000 tokens of page and instructions; that was not reduced here.

## "login flipkart and order …" with no AI: the order runner (7 Nov 2026, late)

**What was seen (terminal log of the next real run, on the previous build):** the sign-in read was right (`signedIn: likely`), but the order died anyway: Gemini was effectively down (gemini-3.6-flash daily quota spent; 3.8-flash and 3.1-flash-lite each hung 10 s), OpenAI had no credit, Groq refused the request size, OpenRouter's key was refused — and the small local model (llama3.2:1b) answered after 40 s "I cannot assist with this request as it involves accessing a website that may be illegal or harmful". Three more faults in that log: (1) Gemini was written off for 30 minutes as "daily allowance used up" because the first error to arrive was one model's quick 429, although the others had only hung; (2) the local model was only kept out of tasks already in progress, not out of the FIRST step of one; (3) every order step needed an AI call, so an AI that is down or over its allowance made ordering impossible.

**What changed.**
 - **Order runner** (`agent/shopRunner.ts`, wired in `AgentEngine.ts` after the site is opened): for Flipkart, Amazon, Myntra, Meesho, AJIO and Nykaa, when the sentence is an order with a product and the page does not say "signed out", the application does the steps itself with the browser tools and no AI call: find the shop's search box and search for the product; pick the result that is really the phone named (all words of the model's name present; never a case, guard or charger, never "currently unavailable", never "… for POCO M6 PRO 5G"; the result with the memory sizes asked for first); open it; choose the variant tile (e.g. "128 GB + 6 GB") if the page opened on another; press "Add to cart" (then open the cart) or, as on Flipkart's phone pages, "Buy at ₹…"; read where that leads (title, what it charges, where it delivers) and say it. It stops at the first place the person decides: a phone that is not sold (it names the closest, e.g. "POCO M6 Pro is not on the shop; closest: M7 Pro, M8x"), a variant the page does not offer, a page whose only button is "Buy now" (held for the yes), a sign-in wall (the person's; no password typed), or the cart / checkout ("I have not paid or placed any order. Say continue …"). It never presses a button that pays or places the order. If a step fails it hands over: the model is told the steps done and carries on from the page.
 - **"check my order status", "track my flipkart order", "cancel the order", "reorder" are not new orders** (nothing is searched).
 - **Small local AI**: also declined at the FIRST step of a request to work a website or account ("login … and order …", when the browser's click/fill tools are asked for), unless the person chose "local only". The plain sentence is said if nothing else answered.
 - **Gemini error classification**: a failed round is given up with the first error that is not a limit on one model (a hang, "high demand"), so one model's spent day no longer rests all of Gemini for 30 minutes.

**Tests.** `tests/shopRunner.test.ts` (23; the real Flipkart's result and button words, the engine with no model call), real-Chrome `tests/live/shoprunner.live.test.ts` on a mock phone shop shaped like Flipkart's (search needs Enter; accessory among the results; a page that opens on the other variant; a painted "Buy at" box) — proves the right phone, the variant tile, "Buy at", the order summary, and that "Place order" is never pressed; `tests/ollamaProvider.test.ts` and `tests/backupAi.test.ts` (+2); whole suite 4,183 passed; 15 deliberate breakages of the runner and AI fixes caught (four survived at first; four tests added). **NOT verified:** the real Flipkart in Rakesh's own signed-in Chrome (whether the real "Buy at" box starts checkout, what the real checkout page shows, the real search box label); a real order; Amazon, Myntra, Meesho, AJIO, Nykaa (same steps, never run on their pages). The next steps after the checkout page (address, delivery, payment) still need the AI.

## Flipkart's real buttons, and a page that is still loading (7 Nov 2026, night)

**What was seen (terminal of the run on the order-runner build):** "That took more steps than I can do in one go" — Eya's own out-of-steps message. The log: the runner did two steps (search, open the phone) and handed over; the model then spent 16 steps and 340,000 tokens looking for "Buy at ₹14,249" and "Buy now". Eya's automatic screen look worked and said why: the product page was **still loading** (a spinner covers it, no buttons yet) when it was read, and later "a large yellow 'Buy now at ₹14,999' button in the bottom right" that she could still not click.

**Causes (found on the real Flipkart page at desktop width, signed out):** (1) the runner read the product page once, at the moment it was a loading screen; (2) the extension's button-box detection (0.11.7) looked for a background COLOUR, but Flipkart's blue "Buy at ₹14,249" box is painted with a background IMAGE six levels above the words, and the yellow "Buy now" button's colour is a separate layer BEHIND the words, so no colour is on the element or any parent; the mock page used a plain colour, so the tests passed and the real page failed (mock ≠ real again); (3) "Buy at ₹14,249" appears to be an offers panel (it says "Apply offers for maximum savings"); the yellow "Buy now" is what starts the checkout.

**What changed.**
 - **Extension 0.11.8** (`injected.js`): STRONG button words (Buy now, Buy at …, Add to cart, Go to cart, Checkout, Place order, Proceed to …, Pay now, Order now) on a short leaf text are a button whatever it is painted with; WEAK words (Continue, Login, Sign in, Next, Submit …) still need a painted box, now a colour OR an image on the element or up to eight parents. Run on the real Flipkart page in a browser: it finds exactly "Buy at ₹14,249" and "Buy now" and nothing else ("Buy together" is not matched). **Reload the extension in Chrome AND Edge** (Edge was still on 0.11.6).
 - **Runner** (`shopRunner.ts`): after opening the phone it reads the page again, every 1.3 s for up to about 10 s, until it has buttons; presses Add to cart, else "Buy now" on Flipkart and Amazon (only there: it opens the checkout, the person's own "order …" is the yes; passes `confirm: true`), else "Buy at …"; reads the page again after each press until it has changed (a loading mask, then the checkout / cart / sign-in) and a button that changed nothing is not taken for success: the next is tried, and if none moves the page the model is handed over to. On other shops "Buy now" is still asked first. Paying and placing the order are never pressed.
 - Test pages now copy the real shapes: image-painted box five levels above the words, a yellow button painted by a separate layer, a "Buy at" box that only opens an offers panel, a 2.5 s loading screen.

**Tests.** `tests/shopRunner.test.ts` (28), real-Chrome `ctabox` (4) and `shoprunner` (1, with the loading screen and the offers box), unit suite 4,189 passed, 15 real-Chrome suites green, 26 deliberate breakages caught in this and the previous round's runner/extension work (one survived — a fixture with the paint one level up — and was fixed). **NOT verified:** the real signed-in Flipkart (what "Buy now" opens there; a synthetic click on it, signed out, raised Flipkart's loading overlay, which is the same signal the real run saw), the real checkout page, the other shops.

## The checkout: no "shall I proceed", one question about paying, one confirmation (7 Nov 2026, night)

**What was seen:** a run on the 0.11.8 build ended in three questions and an out-of-stock: "Shall I proceed with the order?" → yes → "Shall I go ahead and confirm the purchase?" → yes → "out of stock for your delivery address in Makthal". Rakesh: ask only at payment, and then which way (UPI, cash on delivery …); if several delivery addresses are saved, ask which / let me change; do not act blindly; and it must not take minutes — an order within a minute. The terminal showed why: the runner had stopped at its FIRST step (the search box was "not visible" 60 ms after the page opened — it is there a few seconds later), so the AI did the order by itself; Flipkart's "Buy now" tripped the spend gate, and the AI turned each gate into a "shall I proceed?" question (and the second "yes" was a new request that no longer knew what the first was for).

**What changed.**
 - **The search box is waited for** (`shopRunner.ts`): three more tries 1.3 s apart, clicking into the box before the later ones; only then is the AI handed the task.
 - **The checkout is walked by the application** (`checkoutRunner.ts`, no AI) after "Buy now": it reads which step is open (sign-in, item cannot be delivered, delivery address, order summary, payment) and passes the free ones (the address already chosen: "Deliver Here"; the order summary's "Continue"). It stops only where the person must say something:
   - the sign-in (theirs);
   - an item that cannot be delivered to the address: said at once ("… is not available for delivery to Makthal 509208 … another saved address, another variant or another phone"), before anything is bought;
   - several saved addresses: they are listed and the person says the number or a place;
   - the payment step: ONE question, how to pay, listing the ways the page really offers (UPI, Cash on Delivery, Card, Net Banking …), with the item, the total and the address.
 - **The person's answers are understood with no AI** (the engine keeps the checkout that waits, 20 minutes; only a short message, at most 8 words, is read as the answer; anything longer is another request): "UPI" / "cash on delivery" chooses it and asks ONE confirmation with the item, total, address and method; a clear "yes" presses the button that pays or places the order (once, with confirm); "no" stops with nothing placed; a card is the person's to type (Eya never types card numbers, expiry, CVV or PINs); UPI is approved in their own UPI app; if the page after the press shows no order confirmation she says so instead of "done".
 - **If the AI has to take over**, it is told: do not ask "shall I proceed", ask which address only if several, ask ONE question how to pay and ONE confirmation before the click that pays, never type card numbers, be quick.

**Tests.** `tests/checkoutRunner.test.ts` (23), `tests/shopRunner.test.ts` (31, including the whole conversation through the engine with no AI call: order → how to pay → "UPI" → one confirmation → "yes" → placed), real-Chrome `tests/live/shoprunner.live.test.ts` (search → right phone → variant after a loading screen → Buy now → address → summary → asks how to pay → "cash on delivery" → confirmation → "yes" → the pay button pressed once, `confirm`, order placed; nothing paid before), unit suite 4,215 passed, 16 deliberate breakages caught (one survived: a long-message test that could pass by accident; fixed). **NOT verified:** the real Flipkart checkout signed in — the stage detection reads headings, button names ("Deliver Here", "Continue", "Pay …", "Place order") and short lines of text as Flipkart lists them, and its real saved-address and payment pages may differ; where a page is not recognised she hands over to the AI with the steps done. Time: with no AI the steps are a few seconds each (search ~2 s, product page 1–10 s while it loads, each checkout step ~1–2 s); a whole order to the payment question should take well under a minute, if Flipkart's own pages load.

## Live chat: a chat nobody is running no longer stays "running" for hours (7 Nov 2026, night)

**What was seen:** "live chat is not working properly: we said if there is no reply for more than 15 minutes it should stop by itself, but it was continuously 'live' for 15 hours, even after I closed Eya, and it was not sending anything. Then when I asked for a live chat with another number: 'A live chat with +91 90034 88125 is already running. Stop it before starting another.'" The saved file of an older run showed the cause: `state: PAUSED, pause: restart`.

**Causes.** (1) Only a chat that is being watched has a quiet-time clock; a chat that was PAUSED (for any reason, and a chat restored after a restart always comes back paused) had none, so it stayed "running" for ever — and "running" meant a new live chat was refused. (2) A chat whose turn it was not to wait (their message could not be answered because the AI was out of allowance, a reply nobody approved, a step that never finished) was re-checked every 30 seconds and never ended. (3) A restart brought back a session that was written hours ago. (4) The start message said "no limit: it goes on until you pause or stop it" without the 15-minute rule.

**What changed** (`LiveChatController.ts`, `LiveChatStore.ts`, `auto.ts`):
 - **A paused live chat ends by itself**: 15 minutes when it paused by itself (the browser was lost, the chat was closed or left, Eya was restarted), 60 minutes when the person paused it (they chose to hold it). It says so ("Stopped by itself: the live chat with … was paused for N minutes") and alerts.
 - **A chat that cannot move ends too**: with no movement for twice the quiet time (30 minutes) — their message waiting because the AI cannot answer, or a step that never finished — or four times (60 minutes) when a reply is waiting for the person's approval. The reason is in the notice ("…a message of theirs was waiting and I could not answer it (the AI may be out of allowance)").
 - **A restart does not bring back an old chat**: the store now writes when the chat was last saved; a chat saved more than 15 minutes ago, or by an older version that wrote no time, is not restored (it is ended, its words removed). A chat saved a few minutes ago comes back paused with its own 15-minute limit.
 - **Starting a new live chat replaces a dead one**: a paused chat, or one that has done nothing for the whole quiet time, is ended and the new one starts, and she says so ("I ended the earlier live chat with … (it was paused)"). A chat that is really running is not replaced: she says how long ago it was active, that one live chat runs at a time, and that "stop live chat" then asking again starts the new one at once.
 - The start message now says "…until you pause or stop it, or until they have stayed quiet for fifteen minutes".

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatIdle.test.ts` (20; the old tests that pinned "waits for ever" were changed to the new rule; 10 new ones for restore, paused, stuck and replace), `liveChat.test.ts` and `liveChatRealRun.test.ts` (the refusal wording), unit suite 4,223 passed, real-Chrome live chat suites (28 + 10) green, 10 deliberate breakages caught. **NOT done / NOT verified:** two live chats AT THE SAME TIME (the controller is built around one chat in one WhatsApp tab; a second needs its own tab and its own state — a redesign, not a fix); whether another task that opens a site in the same Chrome takes the front tab from WhatsApp and pauses the live chat (the extension opens other sites in their own new tab and never steers the user's own tab, but the new tab becomes the one in front; not observed in a log); the real saved file of the stuck 15-hour chat was in the app's own folder, which could not be read from here.

## Messaging: the message is written properly, shown, sent on a yes — and a yes is understood (7 Nov 2026, night)

**What was seen:** "+91 98765 08501 msg this person, Rakesh told me he will call u night after having dinner, until he said take care, and he asking u, u want anything for diwali" → "Found +91 98765 08501, number ending in 501. Send this message to this chat?" → "ya" → the same question → "yes send" → the same question → "why u asking me again" → "I have to ask for your confirmation…" and the same question. The person wanted the note refined into a proper message, and sent when they say so.

**Causes.** (1) The local parser did not read the sentence (the number first, then "msg this person, …"; and a separator word in the text, "asking", made it give up), so it went to the AI, which typed the note as it was said and clicked Send; the gate's question did not show the message. (2) "ya" was not a yes in the word list, so the question was dropped; "yes send" and the cross reply reached the AI as new requests, which asked the same question again — a yes to a question the AI had asked was never carried out by anything but the AI, and it did not. (3) Nothing rewrote the note.

**What changed.**
 - **The sentence is read** (`chatIntents.ts`): "<number> msg/message/text this person / him / her, <what to say>" is a message to that number; a separator word that gives no usable name no longer ends the reading.
 - **The note is written properly** (`local/messageRefine.ts`): when what was said is a note about what to tell them (reported speech: "told me", "tell him", "he asking"; chat shorthand: u, ur, pls, msg), the AI writes the message (faithful: same facts, same point of view, no additions, ≤ 60 words, spelling and grammar fixed); after 15 s, or if the AI is out of allowance, a rule-based tidy is used (u -> you, "he asking" -> "he is asking", capitals, full stop), so there is always a readable message. A message that is already a message ("hi", "I'll be late") is left exactly as said.
 - **It is shown and waits for the person**: "Found <chat>. I wrote it like this: "…" Send it? Say yes to send, or tell me what to change." — and a message Eya wrote is never sent just because the request was to send (that shortcut is only for words the person said in full). "make it shorter" / "add that …" / "change it to …" writes it again, types it again (replacing the box), shows it, and asks again.
 - **A yes is understood** (`parseYesNo`): ya, yaa, yess, yes send, haan, avunu, ok send it, okk, k and similar; not "yes but change it" and not "ok now go". A cross "send, why are you asking me again" with no "don't/no/stop" in it is a yes to the send that is waiting.
 - **A yes to a question the AI asked is carried out by the application**: the send gate now remembers the message typed in a chat's message box and that it asked about it; the question shows the message ("Send "…" to this chat?"); and the person's yes (or no) is answered by the application, which presses Send in the chosen chat (the wrong-chat check still applies) and checks it went — the AI can no longer ask the same question for ever.

**Tests.** `tests/messageRefine.test.ts` (new, 11), `tests/chatCommands.test.ts` (+14: the sentence, the written message shown, "ya", the yes spellings, the cross yes, "don't send…", the change, "no", an already-written message untouched, the production setting, the AI's own question and "ya"/"no"/a stray yes), real-Chrome `chatflow` (23, two questions now show the message), unit suite 4,247 passed, 12 deliberate breakages caught (one survived until a test used the production setting). **NOT verified:** the real WhatsApp page and a real AI writing the message (tests use a mock chat and a fake writer; the fallback tidy is what an AI-less run produces); how well the small local model refines (it is tried within 15 s, else the tidy); a message in a language other than English is only tidied by the AI, not by rule.

## Live chat: short replies like a person, asking who they are, and several chats at once (7 Nov 2026, night)

**What was asked:** "prevent Eya sending big big messages in live chat — chat like a real human, based on that person's feelings and what they mean; ask before the live chat who the person is (friend, sir, lover…) and talk accordingly; and be able to do multiple live chats."

**Short, in code and not only in the prompt** (`livechat/style.ts`, `draft.ts`). A prompt alone does not hold (the small local model ignores it), so every reply that is going to be sent — the opening, a check-in, an answer — goes through `fitLength` at the exit of `generateDraft/Opening/Nudge`: the limit follows the person (twice the words they wrote plus ten, never under 16 and never over 24 — 30 for a sir/client chat; an opening gets 28, a check-in 18), at most three sentences, cut at a sentence end, a closing question kept. The prompts (cloud and the 1B local one) also carry a feelings rule (answer the feeling first — worried, happy, angry, joking — then the point) and the relation's way of speaking.

**Who they are** (`LiveRelation`: friend, partner, family, senior (sir / madam / boss), client, other). Read from the words ("my girlfriend", "sir", "close friend…"), or remembered from last time, or **asked once before the live chat starts**: "Who is …8501 to you? Say friend, family, sir or boss, partner… I'll chat the way that fits, short and natural like a real person." The answer starts the chat. `RelationBook` keeps a hash of the number or name (never the number or name) → relation, so the same person in any spelling of the number is not asked again. An answer that names nobody ("what is the weather") lapses and is an ordinary request; "no" cancels. The Live chat page has a "Who are they to you?" select as well.

**Several live chats at once** (`livechat/LiveChatHub.ts`; Settings → "Several live chats at once", on by default, up to five). WhatsApp Web shows one chat at a time, so this is turn-taking, honestly: one chat is in front and answers at once, the others are put on *standby* (paused with the reason `standby`: no notice, no alert, no message). A chat gives its turn up only when nothing is going on in it (`settled()`: watching, no unanswered message, nothing being written or sent, no reply waiting for the person) and after a dwell of 25 s (75 s when someone spoke in the last 90 s), then the next one on standby is brought forward. A chat on standby that nobody takes up is ended after twice the quiet time. "stop all live chats", stop one (page button or `stop {chat}`), "show this one" (`focus {chat}`); starting the same chat twice answers with the running one; a sixth is refused with the reason; after a stop the next one takes the turn. Each controller has its own store file (`live-chat.json`, `live-chat-2.json`…). With the setting off a second request behaves as it always did (one live chat; the old one is ended if it is dead, refused if it is running).

**Tests.** `tests/liveChatStyle.test.ts` (16), `liveChatRelationAsk.test.ts` (12), `liveChatHub.test.ts` (17), `liveChatIdle.test.ts` (+3 for standby), the older live-chat tests updated for the length limits; mutation checks on the length cut, the prompts, the question, the book, and every rule of the hub.

**Not verified.** Real WhatsApp Web switching between chats in rotation (a mock page only). Rotation changes which chat is open, so using WhatsApp by hand while several live chats run will fight with it. Real AI tone for a given relationship is prompted and cut to length by code, but not judged on a real person. Another task that opens a browser tab may pause a live chat (not seen).

## Lock, restart and shutdown

`lock_screen` calls Windows' own `LockWorkStation` (`user32.dll`, via `rundll32.exe user32.dll,LockWorkStation` — the same mechanism Microsoft documents for this), then confirms it actually worked by checking that `LogonUI.exe`, the real lock-screen process, appears afterward — not just that the request was sent. `restart_computer` and `shutdown_computer` use Windows' own `shutdown.exe` (`/r` or `/s`, deliberately without `/f`, so an application with unsaved work gets asked to close first rather than force-killed) behind the same ask-first `confirm: true` gate every other destructive tool uses, with a short 5-second delay before either fires so Eya's own spoken "Done." has time to actually finish playing first.

**Honestly, not fired live.** Locking the screen wasn't tested live without asking first, since it would interrupt whatever the user is doing without warning. Restarting and shutting down were built and fully unit-tested with fakes, but a live end-to-end fire — even the safe "schedule it an hour out, then immediately cancel it" trick — was refused outright by this environment's own safety classifier as a real-world system transaction, and rightly so; actually rebooting or shutting down the machine autonomously is exactly the kind of action that should need the user's own hands on it, not an agent's.

## A Recycle Bin gotcha: hidden file extensions

Windows Explorer's Shell COM interface (`Shell.Application`, `Namespace(10)`) exposes a recycled item's display name through its `.Name` property — which silently drops the extension whenever Explorer's own "hide extensions for known file types" setting is on, so a file actually named `report.pdf` gets reported back as just `report`. That broke exact-name lookups (asking to restore `report.pdf` wouldn't match a listed `report`) and was only caught live, not by a unit test, since the fakes used in testing never had that Explorer setting to leak. Fixed at the source, by reading the `System.FileName` extended property instead (reliable, unaffected by that display setting), plus a belt-and-braces fallback in the matching logic itself that also tries the name with its extension stripped either way.

## Roadmap

Two large continuation phases have been requested and scoped down to what could be built and genuinely verified, rather than attempted whole. Delivered: file operations + confirm-before-destroy + reference resolution (phase 2), then smart file search (date/size/type filters, multi-match disambiguation, a numbered "search session" so "the second one" resolves), `close_file` (by window title, not full UI Automation), and a handful of Windows system controls (Settings pages, exact volume, exact brightness) (phase 3), a recycle-vs-permanent delete choice with a consistent "Done." completion reply (phase 4), full Recycle Bin management (list, count, search, restore one or all, permanently delete, empty — all against the real Windows Recycle Bin) plus five additional male voice options (phase 5), a whole-PC default search scope for finding, opening, playing and reading files — every connected drive, not just Downloads/Desktop/Documents (phase 6), real lock/restart/shutdown plus Start-Menu-wide application discovery with a known-web-app fallback (phase 7), and real, DOM-aware website navigation (open/inspect/click/fill on a page, never a search or an invented URL in its place) (phase 8), through to a loop guard (phase 11), a search fallback for when Gemini's own search allowance is spent (phase 12), and working in the user's own signed-in browser through a browser extension with a locally-authenticated bridge, tab reuse, download tracking, CAPTCHA/MFA pause, and confirmation before purchases, sends and deletes (phase 13).

Not built: letting Eya *control* the screen from the Live Screen page (the **Control** button there is disabled; her screen tools `screen_elements` / `screen_click` can click by voice or typing, with their confirmations, but not from that page), and file actions that change things from the Files page (rename, move, delete, send — those stay with the voice and typed tools, which ask before anything destructive).

Deliberately not attempted, because each is a substantial subsystem needing its own real testing (and in two cases, a considered no rather than a "later"):

1. A Gemini Computer Use (screenshot-driven) fallback for pages whose structure the DOM can't describe, a scroll tool, and trusted (real-gesture) clicks — the last would need a permission the browser extension deliberately declines.
2. A larger rewrite into a separate world-state / decision / verification / recovery layer: the Gemini function-calling loop already does observe → decide → act → observe with the real page each turn, so a second layer was judged redundant rather than useful. An "Agent Inspector" screen is not built either (everything it would show is already in the logs and each tool result).
3. Windows UI Automation proper (HWND/focus-event tracking, `WindowPattern.Close`), for richer window/document association than the current title-matching `close_file` gives, and as an alternative path for a page element DOM inspection alone can't identify.
4. A Gemini Computer Use (screenshot-driven) fallback for when neither DOM inspection nor UI Automation can find what it needs.
5. **Wi-Fi/Bluetooth on/off — a deliberate no, not a "later".** Toggling the Wi-Fi adapter risks cutting off the machine Eya runs on; toggling Bluetooth risks dropping a wireless mouse/keyboard; both typically need administrator rights besides. Their Settings pages still open on request.
6. A "trusted mode" setting that skips confirmation for destructive actions (off by default; not started).
7. A task queue with cancellation ("stop", "never mind") and long-running/async tool handling.
8. An audit log of every action taken; detecting a file the user opened manually, outside Eya, and associating it automatically.
9. A trained (not synthetic-voice-tuned) wake word, once real usage data exists.
10. Packaging the app for distribution (the installer exists; see "Installing Eya").
